CoHA of Cyclic Quivers and an Integral Form of Affine Yangians
Shivang Jindal

Abstract
. We calculate the deformed and non-deformed cohomological Hall algebra (CoHA) of the preprojective algebra for the case of cyclic quivers by studying the Kontsevich-Soibelman CoHA and using tools from cohomological Donaldson-Thomas theory. We show that for the cyclic quiver of length K K , this algebra is the universal enveloping algebra of the positive half of a certain extension of matrix differential operators on ℂ ∗ \mathbb{C}^{*} , while its deformation gives a positive half of an explicit integral form of Guay’s Affine Yangian 𝒴 ¨ ℏ 1 , ℏ 2 ​ ( 𝔤 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( K ) ) \ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K)) . By the main theorems of [ 6 ] and [ 82 ] , we also determine the Maulik-Okounkov Yangian for the case of cyclic quivers. Furthermore, we explain the construction of factorization coproduct, provide evidence for the strong rationality conjecture, calculate the spherical subalgebra of the non-deformed CoHA for any quiver without loops, recover results about the CoHA of compactly supported semistable sheaves on the minimal resolution of the Kleinian singularity ℂ 2 / ℤ K \mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K} and identify a commutative algebra inside the additive shuffle algebra associated to the cyclic quiver. We end by conjecturally relating the obtained integral form with the algebra defined by Gaiotto-Rapčák-Zhou, in the context of twisted M-theory.



1.
Introduction

Given a quiver
Q
and potential
W\in\mathbb{C}Q/[\mathbb{C}Q,\mathbb{C}Q]
, where
\mathbb{C}Q
denotes the path algebra, in
[
48
]
Kontsevich and Soibelman defined a cohomological Hall algebra structure on the critical cohomology

\mathcal{A}_{Q,W}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HH(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}^{\mathrm{vir}})

as a mathematical definition of the algebra of BPS states. Here
\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}
is the vanishing cycle functor associated to the function
\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}
on the moduli space of
\mathbf{d}
dimensional representations
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
of the quiver
Q
. The main result of this thesis calculates the algebra and its deformations
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W}
for the quiver
\widetilde{Q^{K}}
and potential
\widetilde{W^{K}}
as defined in Example
1
. This choice of quiver with potential, often called the tripled quiver with canonical potential, provides a presentation of the moduli space of representations of the preprojective algebra of the cyclic quiver
Q^{K}
, equipped with an endomorphism, as a global critical locus. This gives rise to new integral forms of affine Yangians and Lie algebras, which are of independent interest. We start by explaining our main motivations, coming from enumerative geometry and the local geometry of the moduli space of objects in 2-Calabi-Yau categories.

1.1.
Noncommutative Donaldson-Thomas theory

Given any projective 3-Calabi-Yau (CY) variety
X
, indivisible Chern class
\alpha\in\HH^{\textrm{even}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and a generic stability condition
\zeta
, the Donaldson-Thomas invariants
\DT^{\zeta}_{\alpha}
defined in
[
85
]
are virtual counts of semistable sheaves on
X
of given Chern class
\alpha
. They are defined by taking the degree of the virtual fundamental class of the moduli space of sheaves
\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\alpha}(X)
. Later, given any scheme
Y
, Behrend defined a constructible function
\nu_{Y}\colon Y\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
and showed that
\DT^{\zeta}_{\alpha}
is precisely the weighted Euler characteristic
\chi(\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\alpha}(X),\nu_{\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\alpha}(X)})

[
4
]
[Theorem 4.18]. When
Y
can be written as the critical locus of a function
f:\widetilde{Y}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
where
\widetilde{Y}
is smooth, then there exists the vanishing cycle sheaf
\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}
which satisfies (see
[
45
]
[2.1] for details)

\sum(-1)^{i}\dim\HH^{i}(\widetilde{Y},\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})=\chi(Y,v_{Y}).

Thus, the cohomology

\HH(\widetilde{Y},\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

could be understood as a categorification of the DT invariant.

For any 3CY variety
X
, the moduli space
\mathfrak{M}^{\zeta}_{\alpha}(X)
can rarely be written as a global critical locus. However, by the result of
[
86
]
analytically locally these moduli spaces can be written as the moduli space of representations of a Jacobi algebra
\Jac(Q,W)
(Definition
3.7
)
1
1

1

To be more precise, Toda’s theorem gives an analytic potential and so we need to consider an analytic Jacobi algebra
\Jac\{Q,W\}
as defined in
[
21
]
[Definition 2.7].
. Furthermore, the moduli stack of representations of a Jacobi algebra can be written as the critical locus of a smooth function
\Tr(W)
on a smooth moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
. The study of the DT invariants of a non-compact Calabi–Yau threefold via virtual counts of moduli of
\Jac(Q,W)
modules, where
\Jac(Q,W)
is the Jacobi algebra derived equivalent to
X
, was initiated by Szendrői in the case of the conifold
[
84
]
. For any quiver
Q
, we can define the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
with the canonical cubic potential
\widetilde{{W}}
(Example
1
). When
X=\mathbb{C}^{3}
the moduli stack
\Coh_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
of torsion sheaves of length
n
can be described as a moduli stack of
n
dimensional representations of the Jacobi algebra of the tripled Jordan quiver
\widetilde{Q_{\Jor}}
with canonical potential
\widetilde{W}_{\Jor}:=x[y,z]
.

The example concerning the main result of this paper captures the geometry of Kleinian surfaces. Let
S_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
be the minimal resolution of the quotient singularity where the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
is given by
(x,y)\rightarrow(\omega x,\omega^{-1}y)
and
\omega^{K+1}=1
is a primitive root of unity. We then have an equivalence of derived categories

(1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\Coh(S_{K}\times\mathbb{C}))\simeq\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\Jac(\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}))

where
Q^{K}
is the cyclic quiver with
K+1
vertices. The Jacobi algebra
\Jac(\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}})
is the
3
CY completion of the preprojective algebra
\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}}

[
44
]
.

1.2.
Cohomological Hall algebras of preprojective algebras

There is a deep relationship between cohomological Donaldson-Thomas Theory of objects in the 3CY completion
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
of a 2CY category
\mathcal{C}
and the Borel-Moore homology of objects in
\mathcal{C}
which goes under the name of dimensional reduction (
[
46
]
,
[
16
]
,
[
17
]
[Appendix]). In particular, the dimensional reduction theorem implies that for any quiver
Q
, there is an isomorphism
2
2

2

Note that the quotient stack
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})
is not smooth, so here by
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}
we mean the shifted complex
\mathbb{Q}[-2(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})]
, where
(\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt,\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt)
denotes the Euler form, as defined in Definition
3.4
.

(2)

\HH(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q}),\pPhi_{\Tr(\widetilde{W})}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q})})\simeq\HH^{\textrm{BM}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

The mixed Hodge structure on the right, i.e. on the BM homology of the stack of representations of the preprojective algebra
\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}
, plays an important role in multiple branches of mathematics. In particular, by the Ext quiver construction, the stack of representations of the preprojective algebra étale locally models the geometry of the stack of objects in arbitrary 2CY categories possessing good moduli spaces
[
20
]
. Examples of such moduli spaces include Higgs bundles on smooth projective curves, moduli of local systems on Riemann surfaces, and coherent sheaves on K3 surfaces. The first two examples also make studying this space a valuable tool in the study of non-abelian Hodge theory, while on the other hand, there is an algebra structure on

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

which has been defined in the context of equivariant cohomology for the case of Jordan quiver
[
79
]
in the context of AGT conjecture and later defined for arbitrary quivers in
[
94
]
. This algebra acts on the cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties by raising operators and, in some sense, is the biggest possible algebra acting on the cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties, making it an important object in geometric representation theory.
It is shown in
[
78
]
[Appendix A] and
[
95
]
that up to a sign twist, the isomorphism in (
2
) in fact preserves the algebra structure and thus is an isomorphism of algebras. Thus studying
\mathcal{A}_{Q,W}
completely determines
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}}
.

2.
Results

2.1.
Integral matrix
\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}
Lie algebras

Let
\hbar
be a formal variable and let
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
be the algebra of
\hbar\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt
differential operators on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
. It is defined as a unital associative
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear algebra generated by
z^{\pm 1},D
subject to the relations:

(3)

Dz=z(D+\hbar),\ z^{+1}z^{-1}=z^{-1}z^{+1}=1.

We will view
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
as a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebra with the commutator Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]
, coming from the associative algebra structure on
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
. A central extension of the Lie algebra
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
, after specialization at
\hbar=1
, is called
\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}
Lie algebra. The
\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}
Lie algebra was first introduced in
[
39
]
. Its connection with the vertex algebras
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
for central charge
N
, where
N
is any natural number, has been developed in
[
29
]

[
40
]
. Their matrix
generalization was considered in
[
3
]
(see also
[
83
]
,
[
26
]
and
[
13
]
for a more physical perspective).

Let
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
be the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear associative algebra of
K\times K
matrices with values in
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
, i.e. for any
f(z,D)\otimes X
and
g(z,D)\otimes Y
in
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
where
f(z,D),g(z,D)\in D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
and
X,Y\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
, the multiplication is given by

(f(z,D)\otimes X)(g(z,D)\otimes Y)=f(z,D)g(z,D)\otimes XY.

We view
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
as a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebra with the commutator Lie bracket coming from the associative algebra structure on
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
, i.e. Lie bracket is given by

[f(z,D)\otimes X,g(z,D)\otimes Y]=(f(z,D)g(z,D))\otimes XY-(g(z,D)f(z,D))\otimes YX

for any
f(z,D)\otimes X,g(z,D)\otimes Y
in
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
. The elements

T_{k,a}(X)=z^{k}D^{a}\otimes X\textrm{ where }k\in\mathbb{Z},a\geq 0,X\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K}

form a spanning subset of
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
. We slightly extend this Lie algebra by defining an integral form.

Definition 2.1
.

Let

\mathcal{W}_{K}\subset(D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]

be the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear subspace spanned by
T_{k,a}(X),X\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
and

t_{k,a}:=T_{k,a}(1)/\hbar

where
k\in\mathbb{Z},a\geq 0,X\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
and by
1
, we mean the identity matrix
\mathrm{Id}\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K}
.

The subspace
\mathcal{W}_{K}\subset(D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]
in fact forms a Lie subalgebra (Proposition
15.1
).
We remark that the Lie algebra
\mathcal{W}_{K}
is also considered in
[
30
]
. We consider the classical limit as
\hbar\rightarrow 0
of
\mathcal{W}_{K}
.

Let
\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{T}^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
be the ring of functions on
\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*}
. We denote by
\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
, the Lie algebra on
\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
, where the Lie bracket is given by the standard Poisson bracket
\{\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt,\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\}
. Let
\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
be the Lie algebra with the Lie bracket given by

[f\otimes X,g\otimes Y]=fg\otimes[X,Y]

for any
X,Y\in\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
and
f,g\in\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
.

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
acts on
\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
via the Poisson bracket with the first tensor product, i.e.

f\cdot(g\otimes X):=\{f,g\}\otimes X,

for any
f\in\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
and
g\otimes X\in\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
. Let

\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K})

be the Lie algebra given by the semidirect product of
\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})
with
\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
. The classical limit
\hbar\rightarrow 0
of
\mathcal{W}_{K}
is exactly the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K})
, i.e. There is an isomorphism of Lie algebras

(\mathcal{W}_{K})/(\hbar=0)\simeq\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}).

In Proposition
15.3
, we present this Lie algebra via generators and relations. Our first main result describes the cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
as a universal enveloping algebra of the positive half of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K})
.

Theorem A

(Theorem
16.6
)
.

Let
K\geq 1
. There is an isomorphism of Lie algebras

\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq(\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K+1}))^{+}

between the the affinized BPS Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
for the cyclic quiver (Section
9.2
) and the positive half
(\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}))^{+}\subset\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K})
, defined in Definition
15.4
. This gives an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\bU((\mathfrak{po}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\ltimes(\mathcal{O}(\TT^{*}\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K+1}))^{+}),

where
\chi
is a sign twist, defined in Section
6.4.1
.

It is also possible to recover the positive half of
\mathcal{W}_{K}
itself, without taking the classical limit. To do so, we consider the deformed cohomological Hall algebra. For any torus
T
acting on the quiver
\widetilde{Q^{K}}
, leaving the potential
\widetilde{W^{K}}
invariant, there is a cohomological Hall algebra (Section
6
) structure on the equivariant vanishing cycle cohomology

\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{K+1}}\HH_{T}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q^{K}}),\pPhi_{\Tr(\widetilde{W^{K}})}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q^{K}})}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

Our next main result, calculates this algebra for
T=\mathbb{C}^{*}
, whose action on
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q^{K}})
, leaves the added loops
\omega_{i}
invariant for all
i\in Q_{0}
. We set up a bit more notation. For any commutative ring
R
and a
R
linear Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, its tensor algebra is defined to be

T_{R}(\mathfrak{g}):=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\underbrace{\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{R}\dots\otimes_{R}\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{m times }},

and correspondingly, the universal enveloping algebra over
R
is defined to be the quotient

\bU_{R}(\mathfrak{g}):=T_{R}(\mathfrak{g})/\langle ab-ba-[a,b]\mid a,b\in\mathfrak{g}\rangle.

Theorem B

(Theorem
19.3
)
.

For
K\geq 1
, let
\mathbb{C}^{*}
act on representations of the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q^{K}}
by acting on all the original arrows
a
with weight
1
, all opposite arrows
a^{*}
with weight
-1
and with weight
0
on added loops
\omega_{i},i\in[0,K]
(Section
8.2
). Then there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebras

\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}

between the deformed affinized BPS Lie algebra, defined in the Section
9.2
and the positive half of an integral form of differential operators on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
valued in matrices, defined in Defintion
15.2
. This gives an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\bU_{\mathbb{C}[h]}((\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+})

where
\chi
is a sign twist, defined in Section
6.4.1
.

Next, we cansider the action of a larger torus
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
, which acts on the added loops
\omega_{i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
non-trivially. This gives rise to quantum groups.

2.2.
Integral form of Yangians

Given a semisimple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, in
[
25
]
Drinfeld defined the Yangian
\mathcal{Y}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
as a deformation of the universal enveloping algebra
\bU(\mathfrak{g}[x])
of the polynomial current Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}[x]
. Yangians form a family of quantum groups related to rational solutions of classical Yang-Baxter equations. For any quiver
Q
, in
[
56
]
, the authors defined the Yangian
\textbf{Y}^{\textrm{MO}}_{Q}
by geometrically defining
R
matrices using stable envelopes on the singular cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties and applying the
FRT
formalism to construct quantum groups. One of Maulik and Okounkov’s motivations was to realise the Quantum cohomology ring as a maximal commutative subalgebra of the Yangian. From the calculations in
[
55
]
for the quiver variety for cyclic quivers, it was clear that one needs slightly larger algebra than the usual affine Yangians for the case of cyclic quivers. They conjectured a strong relation between the quantum cohomology of the Nakajima quiver varieties and the Bethe subalgebras of
\textbf{Y}^{\textrm{MO}}_{Q}
. However, the algebras
\textbf{Y}^{\textrm{MO}}_{Q}
are not known for cases beyond ADE and Jordan quivers. The computation for the Jordan quiver occupies much of the latter half of
[
56
]
, where it is shown to be isomorphic to the affine Yangian
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(1))
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}(1)}
and used in their proof of AGT conjecture. For the ADE quivers
Q
, a central quotient of
\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}
is shown to be isomorphic to the Yangian
\mathcal{Y}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g}_{Q})
, as defined by Drinfeld
[
57
]
.

For any quiver with potential
Q,W
, the cohomological Hall algebras are also known to satisfy a similar property, i.e. there is a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,T}_{Q,W}
such that
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W}\simeq\Sym_{\HH_{T}(\pt)}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,T}_{Q,W}[u])
as graded vector spaces
[
15
]
, allowing us to think of
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W}
as generalized Yangians. For particular choices of torus, it is shown that for ADE quivers, there is an isomorphism of algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\simeq Y^{+}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g}_{Q})
(
[
94
]
+
[
80
]
) and for Jordan quiver, there is an isomorphism of algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q_{\Jor}},\widetilde{W_{\Jor}}}\simeq\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(1))
by (
[
18
]
+
[
76
]
) and
[
79
]
where
+
denotes the positive half. Furthermore, for all tripled quivers with canonical potential
\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}
, there is an isomorphism of algebras

\textbf{Y}^{\textrm{MO},+}_{Q}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}

as proved in
[
6
]
and isomorphism

\textbf{Y}^{\textrm{MO},-}_{Q}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{T,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}

is proved in
[
82
]
, where
\mathcal{A}^{T,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
is a nilpotent cohomological Hall algebra. Here,
T
is an appropriately chosen torus. Strictly speaking, Maulik-Okounkov Yangians are only defined with respect to
T
-equivariant cohomology, where
T
is some torus that scales the natural symplectic form on the Nakajima quiver varieties associated to the quiver
Q
, non-trivially.

We now consider the cyclic quiver
Q^{K}
and choose
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
to be a 2 torus. The spherical subalgebra of the localized
K
-theoretic version of the cohomological Hall algebra
[
73
]
of the preprojective algebra, defined by considering
K
theory instead of cohomology, was considered in
[
69
]
for the case of cyclic quivers. It is shown to be isomorphic to the positive half of the quantum toroidal algebra
\ddot{\bU}_{q,t}(\mathfrak{gl}(K))
. Its rational counterpart, the affine Yangian, has been defined in
[
33
]
to be a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
algebra
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K))
for
K>2
as a deformation of a central extension of
\bU(\mathfrak{sl}_{K}[u^{\pm},v])
. The case when
K=2
was first defined by Kodera in
[
47
]
and also considered by Bershtein-Tsymbaliuk in
[
5
]
. A specialization of this algebra at
\hbar_{1}=\hbar_{2}
has been shown to be isomorphic to a spherical subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
where
\mathbb{C}^{*}\subset\mathbb{T}
acts by scaling the arrows with the same weight in
[
96
]
. However
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
is not spherically generated and this makes computing
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
essentially a new problem. In Proposition
17.11
implies that
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes_{\HH_{\mathbb{T}}(\pt)}\mathrm{Frac}(\HH_{\mathbb{T}}(\pt))\simeq\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K))\otimes_{\HH_{\mathbb{T}}(\pt)}\mathrm{Frac}(\HH_{\mathbb{T}}(\pt))
. Motivated by this, we slightly enlarge the algebra by including the non-spherically generated part. Note that our only addition is to add elements
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{\pm}
to the existing definition of Affine Yangian. We use the conventions in
[
5
]
. We define

Definition 2.2

(CoHA Affine Yangian)
.

Assume
n\geq 2
. Let
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
be the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
algebra generated by
X^{\pm}_{i,r},H_{i,r}
for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{+}^{(r)},\mathfrak{K}_{-}^{(r)}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, with the relations

(R0)

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{-}_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,j}H_{i,r+s}

(R1)

\displaystyle[H_{i,r},H_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=0

for any
n\geq 2
. When
n>2
,

(R2)

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{\pm},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]-[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}]

\displaystyle=-m_{ij}(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}/2)[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]\pm a_{ij}\hbar_{2}/2\{X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}\}

(R3)

\displaystyle[H_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]-[H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}]

\displaystyle=-m_{ij}(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}/2)[H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]\pm a_{ij}\hbar_{2}/2\{H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}\}

(R4)

\displaystyle[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=0\ \textrm{ for all }\ |i-j|>1

(R5)

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2}}[X^{\pm}_{i,r_{\sigma(1)}},[X^{\pm}_{i,r_{\sigma(2)}},X^{\pm}_{i\pm 1,s}]]

\displaystyle=0\ ;\ \ [H_{i,0},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]=\pm a_{ij}X^{\pm}_{j,s}

\displaystyle(\hbar_{1})(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{\pm}=\mathrm{T}^{r}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}[X^{\pm}_{i,0},[X^{\pm}_{i+1,1},[X^{\pm}_{i+2,0},[\cdots,X^{\pm}_{i+n-1,0}]]]]\right)

\displaystyle-\hbar_{2}\mathrm{T}^{r}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}X^{\pm}_{i,0}\left([X^{\pm}_{i+1,0},[X^{\pm}_{i+2,0},[\cdots,X^{\pm}_{i+n-1,0}]]]\right))\right)

for any
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
and
r,s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
where
m_{ij}=-\delta_{i+1,j}+\delta_{i,j+1}
,
a_{ij}=2\delta_{i,j}-\delta_{i,j+1}-\delta_{i,j-1}
. Here
\mathrm{T}
is an operator defined so that
\mathrm{T}(X^{\pm}_{i,r}):=\pm(X^{\pm}_{i,r+1}),\mathrm{T}(H_{i,r})=0
and
\mathrm{T}
is a derivation, i.e.
\mathrm{T}(ab):=\mathrm{T}(a)b+a\mathrm{T}(b)
for any
a,b
. By the curly brackets, we mean
\{a,b\}:=ab+ba
and finally,
S_{2}
is the symmetric group in
2
elements.

When
n=2
, we have the following relations:

(R2.1)

[X^{\pm}_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{i,s}]-[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{i,s+1}]=\pm h_{2}\{X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{i,s}\},

(R2.2)

\begin{split}&[X^{\pm}_{i,r+2},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]-2[X^{\pm}_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}]+[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+2}]=\\
&\hbar_{1}(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})[X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]\mp\hbar_{2}(\{X^{\pm}_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{j,s}\}-\{X^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}\})\ \mathrm{for}\ j\neq i,\end{split}

(R3.1)

[H_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{i,s}]-[H^{\pm}_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{i,s+1}]=\pm h_{2}\{H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{i,s}\},

(R3.2)

\begin{split}&[H_{i,r+2},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]-2[H_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}]+[H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+2}]=\\
&\hbar_{1}(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})[H_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]\mp\hbar_{2}(\{H_{i,r+1},X^{\pm}_{j,s}\}-\{X_{i,r},X^{\pm}_{j,s+1}\})\ \mathrm{for}\ j\neq i,\end{split}

(R4.1)

[H_{i,0},X^{\pm}_{j,s}]=\pm a_{i,j}X^{\pm}_{j,s},\ \ [H_{i,1},X^{\pm}_{i+1,s}]=\mp(2H^{\pm}_{i+1,s+1}+\hbar_{2}\{H_{i,0},X^{\pm}_{i+1,s}\}),

(5.1)

\underset{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}}{\Sym}[X^{\pm}_{i,r_{1}},[X^{\pm}_{i,r_{2}},[X^{\pm}_{i,r_{3}},X^{\pm}_{i+1,s}]]]=0.

\displaystyle(\hbar_{1})(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{\pm}=\mathrm{T}^{r}\left([X^{\pm}_{0,0},X^{\pm}_{1,1}]+[X^{\pm}_{1,0},X^{+}_{0,1}]\right)

\displaystyle-\hbar_{2}\mathrm{T}^{r}\left(X^{\pm}_{0,0}X^{+}_{1,0}+X^{+}_{1,0}X^{+}_{0,0}\right)

Clearly
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1}^{\pm 1},(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})^{\pm 1}]\simeq\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1}^{\pm 1},(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})^{\pm 1}]
, since we have only added elements
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{\pm}
to the originial definition of
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K))
which by relations (
2.2
) and (
2.2
), after multiplication by
(\hbar_{1})(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})
belong to
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
. We then have

Theorem C

(Theorem
17.15
)
.

For
K\geq 1
, let
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
be the 2-torus which acts on representations of the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q^{K}}
by scaling all the original arrows
a
with weight
(1,0)
, all opposite arrows
a^{*}
with weight
(0,1)
and added loops
\omega_{i},i\in[0,K]
with weight
(-1,-1)
(Section
8.2
). Then we have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),+,\CoHA}_{-\hbar_{2},\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}}

where
\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),+,\CoHA}_{-\hbar_{2},\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}}\subset\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),\CoHA}_{-\hbar_{2},\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}}
is the subalgebra generated by
X^{+}_{i,r}
and
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{+}
for
i\in[0,K],r\geq 0
and
\chi
is a sign twist, defined in Section
6.4.1
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
be the subalgebra of
\mathcal{Y}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
which is generated by generators
\langle\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{+},X^{\pm}_{i,r},H_{i,r}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\geq 0\rangle
(So we only exclude the generators
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{-}
for
r\geq 0
). Let
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}=\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}[\langle k_{i,r}\rangle]
be the extended algebra, where we have added central elements
\langle k_{i,r}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\geq 0\rangle
to the list of generators. Then we have

Corollary A

(Theorem
18
)
.

For
K\geq 1
, the Maulik-Okounkov Yangian
\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}}
is isomorphic to an extended integral form of Guay’s affine Yangian. i.e. There is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
linear algebras

\mathbf{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}}\simeq\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{(K+1),\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{-\hbar_{2},\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}}.

Proof Strategy for Theorems A, B, C

We remark that although by taking the classical limit, Theorem C
\implies
Theorem B
\implies
Theorem A, to prove them, we first prove Theorem A. There exists a cocommutative coproduct on
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
which is compatible with the cohomological Hall algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
(Section
9.2
). By the Milnor-Moore theorem,
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\bU(\mathcal{P})
where
\mathcal{P}
is the Lie algebra of the primitive elements with respect to this coproduct. We refer to this Lie algebra as the affinized BPS Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. There exists a filtration, referred to as the perverse filtration
\mathfrak{P}
on
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, given by a decomposition type theorem. The associated graded algebra with respect to this filtration is isomorphic to
\Sym(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}[u])
, where
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is the non-affinized BPS Lie algebra (Section
6.5
). This implies that, as a vector space
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}[u]
, but not as a Lie algebra (Section
8.2
). With the help of an action of the three-dimensional Heisenberg Lie algebra (Section
10
), we show that a much smaller subspace of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, and the relations among them, determine the affinized BPS Lie algebra. Finally, to compute the relations, we construct a geometric action of
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
on the cohomology of the equivariant Hilbert scheme
\Hilb^{\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
and the cohomology of Hilbert scheme of points on the minimal resolution
\Hilb(S_{K})
(Chapter
12
). By identifying the induced action of
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
with the action of the affine Lie Algebra and the infinite-dimensional Heisenberg Lie algebra due to Nakajima, we prove Theorem A in Section
16
.

To calculate
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, we use the embedding into the cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
of the same quiver but without potential. The algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
can be explicitly described in terms of a shuffle product (Section
17.1
). The calculation of the undeformed version gives a minimal set of generators of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
(Proposition
17.6
). We find the image of these generators in
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
(Proposition
17.7
). This allows us to construct a homomorphism of algebras from the positive half of Guay’s affine Yangian to the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
(Section
17.2
). Finally, with the help of the PBW theorem for
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, we prove that this morphism is an the isomorphism, proving Theorem C (Proposition
17.11
and Theorem
17.15
). Finally, we take the classical limit
\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}\rightarrow 0
to calculate the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
deformed algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, proving Theorem B.

2.3.
Spherical subalgebra

The spherical subalgebra, i.e, the subalgebra generated by the smallest possible dimension vectors of the deformed version of the cohomological Hall algebra, has been studied extensively in
[
93
]
. An important tool used in
[
93
]
is embedding into the cohomological Hall algebra of quiver without potential. However, no such injection exists in the non-deformed case (Section
17.1.2
). As an application of general tools from
[
18
]
, developed in Section
10
, we can precisely calculate the spherical part for any quivers without loops.

Theorem D

(Theorem
11.2
)
.

Let
Q
be any quiver without loops. Then there is an isomorphism of algebras

\bU(\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{Q}[D])\simeq\mathcal{SA}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}

where
\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+}
is the positive half of the Kac-Moody Lie algebra (Definition
7.2
) associated with
Q
,
\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{+}[D]
is the current Lie algebra (Definition
10.8
),
\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
is the subalgebra generated by
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\delta_{i}}
for
i\in Q_{0}
and
\chi
is a sign twist, defined in Section
6.4.1
.

We remark that for quivers without loops,
\mathcal{SA}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\chi}=\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}^{\chi}
if and only if the quiver is an orientation of a finite type ADE Dynkin quiver
[
18
]
[Proposition 5.7].

2.4.
Strong rationality conjecture

Given
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
a three-fold flopping contraction with exactly one exceptional fibre, which is isomorphic to a rational curve
C
, one can associate numerous invariants. One is the Gopakumar-Vafa invariant
n_{C,r}
. In
[
21
]
, the author gives a cohomological categorification
\BPS_{r,d}
of these numbers in terms of BPS cohomology of the contraction algebra associated with the flopping curve
C
, where
d
is the Euler characteristic of the coherent sheaves on
X
. The invariance of BPS invariants under the choice of Euler characteristic for
3CY
varieties has been conjectured in the work of Pandharipande and Thomas
[
74
]
. By analogy, the conjecture
[
21
]
[Conjecture 5.9] is the statement that this invariance lifts to an isomorphism of BPS cohomology

\BPS_{r,d}\simeq\BPS_{r,d+1}.

Here, the isomorphism is constructed by defining a Hecke correspondence from the cohomological Hall algebra. In particular, it implies the strong rationality conjectures in
[
87
]
[
74
]
for flopping curves. We can consider a similar Hecke correspondence for the case when
X=S_{K}\times\mathbb{C}
where
S_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
is the minimal resolution of a Kleinian singularity. Then the proof of Theorem C implies that the Hecke correspondence indeed gives an isomorphism between BPS cohomology, as we vary
d
.

Corollary B

(Corollary
16.3.1
)
.

The cohomological lift of the strong rationality conjecture is true for
S_{K}\times\mathbb{C}
.

2.5.
Cohomological Hall algebra of Kleinian surfaces

Given any smooth quasi-projective surface
S
, in
[
42
]
, the authors defined a cohomological Hall algebra structure on the Borel-Moore homology of the stack of compactly supported sheaves on
S
. Furthermore, this structure was upgraded to the categorical level in
[
75
]
. Given any finite subgroup
G\subset\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, let
S_{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/G
be the minimal resolution. Then in
[
24
]
, the authors study the cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{G})
of
\omega
-semistable properly supported sheaves on
S_{G}
with fixed slope
\mu
. They show that there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{G})\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{0}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q_{G}})

where
Q_{G}
is the affine ADE quiver associated to the group
G
by the McKay correspondence. Here
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{0}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})
is the cohomological Hall algebra of slope
0
,
\zeta
-semistable representations of the preprojective algebra
Q
, where the stability condition
\zeta
is explictly determined in terms of
\omega
and
\mu
. Now assume
G=\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
. The main result and the properties of the affinized BPS Lie algebra allow us to explicitly determine
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta^{\prime}}_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}})
as a subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}}}
, for any choice of
\zeta^{\prime}
and
\mu^{\prime}
. In particular, as a corollary, we recover the calculation of the cohomological Hall algebra of
0
-dimensional sheaves
\mathcal{A}(S_{K})
as in
[
60
]
and of
\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})
as in
[
24
]
.

Corollary C
.

(1)

Let
W(S_{K})
be the Lie algebra defined in the Corollary (
16.8.1
). We then have an isomorphism of algebras (Corollary (
16.8.1
))

\mathcal{A}(S_{K})\simeq\bU(W(S_{K})).

(2)

Let
W^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})
be the Lie algebra as defined in the Definition
16.9
. We then have an isomorphism of algebras (Corollary
16.9.1
)

\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})\simeq\bU(W^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})).

2.6.
Shuffle algebra realization and commutative subalgebras

It is an interesting problem to construct the realization of quantum groups in terms of a shuffle algebra, which is an algebra structure on the symmetric polynomial ring with a shuffle product. This idea dates back to the work of Enriques in
[
27
]
. In
[
70
]
, the localized K-theoretic Hall algebras are realized in terms of polynomials in the shuffle algebra satisfying Feigin-Odesskii type wheel conditions
3
3

3

In some references, the shuffle algebra is defined to be the subalgebra of symmetric Laurent polynomials satisfying the wheel conditions. See
[
70
]
[Definition 2.9].
. However, determining the explicit integral form is far from being known. In
[
71
]
, the author described the seminilpotent K-theoretic Hall algebra of the preprojective algebra of the Jordan quiver as a shuffle algebra with polynomials satisfying certain divisibility conditions, giving a shuffle realization of
an integral form of
\ddot{\bU}_{q,t}(\mathfrak{gl}(1))
. In
[
97
]
, the author gives some necessary but not sufficient divisibility conditions in the shuffle algebra associated to the surface to be in the image of the K-theoretic Hall algebra of any smooth projective surface. In
[
88
]
, the author describes an integral form of the Yangian of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}
by divisibility conditions.

In Section
17.7
, we describe the image of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
in the shuffle algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
as a subalgebra generated by certain specific polynomials. We remark that the additive shuffle algebra (without wheel conditions) considered in
[
5
]
[Section 5.2] is isomorphic to the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
after localization (Remark
17.2
).

We now state an interesting corollary of this embedding. By computing the image of a natural trivial Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, we define elements
L_{n},n\geq 1
in the shuffle algebra (Corollary
17.7.1
) such that

Corollary D

(Corollary
17.7.1
)
.

For all
n\geq 1
, let
A^{\mathbb{T}}_{K}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
be the subalgebra generated by
L_{n}
. Then
A^{\mathbb{T}}_{K}\simeq\mathbb{C}[L_{1},L_{2},\cdots]
is a polynomial subalgebra.

For the quantum toroidal algebra
\ddot{\bU}_{q,t}(\mathfrak{gl}(1))
, a commutative polynomial subalgebra is defined in
[
28
]
[Theorem 2.4] and its action on the
K
theory of
\Hilb^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
has been studied in relation to the theory of Macdonald polynomials. Its rational counterpart, has been defined in
[
89
]
[Theorem 7.7], and it can be seen as the image of the commutative Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q_{\Jor}},\widetilde{W}_{\Jor}}
in the shuffle algebra from
[
18
]
[Section 3.13].

2.7.
Algebras in physics literature

In
[
30
]
, for every
K\neq 2
, the authors construct certain double deformed current algebras
A^{K}
as the algebra of gauge-invariant local observables on M2 branes, in the twisted M-theory background. They further construct a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
linear algebra
Y^{K}
by gluing two copies of
A^{K}
. They show that
Y^{K}
is also an integral form of the affine Yangian
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K))
and after quotienting by a central element, this algebra deforms
\mathcal{W}_{K}
. We expect that (see Section
19.1
)

Conjecture 1
.

For any
K>2
, there is an isomorphism of algebras

Y^{K}\simeq\mathcal{Y}^{(K),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}.

This would also explain the expectation in
[
9
]
[Section 1.8] concerning the embedding of the cohomological Hall algebra in Costello’s deformed double current algebra
\mathcal{O}_{\hbar_{1}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\hbar_{2}}(\bullet,K))
, since it is shown in
[
30
]
[Theorem 2] that Costello’s algebra is isomorphic to a particular integral form of the algebra
A^{K}
which contains the positive half of
Y^{K}

[
30
]
[Definition 7.01].

2.9.
Notations

•

All schemes/stacks are considered over
\mathbb{C}
.

•

For any algebraic group
G
,
\text{BG}:=\pt/G
is the stack theoretic quotient, while
\HH_{G}:=\HH(\text{BG},\mathbb{Q})
.

3.
Quivers and Moduli Spaces

In this chapter, we will introduce our main object of study. We first set up some notations.

A quiver is a directed graph
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1},s,t)
, where
Q_{0},Q_{1}
are finite sets and
s,t:Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{0}
. The set
Q_{0}
is the set of vertices, and
Q_{1}
is the set of edges. For every edge
\alpha\in Q_{1}
,
s(\alpha)
is the source of the edge, and
t(\alpha)
is the target of the edge.

Definition 3.1

(Path algebra)
.

Let
\mathbb{C}Q
be the path algebra of
Q
, i.e., the algebra over
\mathbb{C}
having the paths in
Q
as a basis, with multiplication given by concatenation of paths. For each vertex
i\in Q_{0}
, there is a lazy path of length
0
starting and ending at
i
, and we denote by
e_{i}
the resulting element of
\mathbb{C}Q
.

Given a quiver
Q
, the doubled quiver
\overline{Q}
is the quiver obtained by adding a new arrow
a^{*}
for each arrow
a\in Q_{1}
, with
s(a^{*})=t(a)
and
t(a^{*})=s(a)
. We then define

Definition 3.2

(Preprojective algebra)
.

The preprojective algebra is defined as the quotient

\mathrm{\mathrm{\Pi}}_{Q}:=\mathbb{C}\overline{Q}/\langle\sum[a,a^{*}]\rangle.

From now on, by a representation of a quiver, we mean a left
\ CQ
module.

Definition 3.3

(Dimension)
.

A left
\mathbb{C}Q
module
\rho
is said to be of dimension
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
defined by

\mathbf{d}_{i}:=\dim(e_{i}\cdot\rho).

Convention 1
.

For quiver
Q
, and for any
0\leq i\leq|Q_{0}|-1
we set
\delta_{i}=(\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{i\ 0s},1,0,\cdots,0)\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
and for any
m\leq|Q_{0}|-1
, we set

[i,m)=\sum_{j=i}^{j=i+m\mod(|Q_{0}|)}\delta_{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}

is a cyclic string of
0
s and
1
s with
m+1
consecutive
1
s starting from position
i
and remaining terms
0
. Note that
[i,0)=\delta_{i}
and
[i+1,|Q_{0}|-2)=\delta-\delta_{i}
where
\delta=(\underbrace{1,\cdots,1}_{|Q_{0}|\textrm{ times }})
.

Given two representations
\rho,\rho^{\prime}
of dimension
\mathbf{d},\textbf{e}
respectively, we consider the Euler form

(\rho,\rho^{\prime}):=\dim(\Hom(\rho,\rho^{\prime}))-\dim(\Ext^{1}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})),

which in this case, only depends on the dimensions
\mathbf{d},\textbf{e}
.

Definition 3.4

(Euler form)
.

Given a pair of dimension vectors
\mathbf{d},\textbf{e}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
, we define the Euler form

\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d},\textbf{e}):=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbf{d}_{i}\textbf{e}_{i}-\sum_{\alpha\in Q_{1}}\mathbf{d}_{s(\alpha)}\textbf{e}_{t(\alpha)}.

Definition 3.5

(Serre Subcategory)
.

A Serre subcategory
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{C}Q-\mathrm{mod}
such that for every short exact sequence of representations

0\rightarrow\rho_{1}\rightarrow\rho_{2}\rightarrow\rho_{3}\rightarrow 0,

\rho_{2}
is in
\mathcal{S}
if and only if
\rho_{1},\rho_{3}
are in
\mathcal{S}
.

Definition 3.6

(Potential)
.

A potential
4
4

4

Also called superpotential in physics terminology
on a quiver
Q
is an element
W\in\mathbb{C}Q/[\mathbb{C}Q,\mathbb{C}Q]
. A potential is given by a linear combination of cyclic words in
Q
, where two cyclic words are considered to be the same if one can be cyclically permuted to be the other. If
W
is a single cyclic word and
a\in Q_{1}
, then we define

\partial W/\partial a=\sum_{W=cac^{\prime}}c^{\prime}c

And we extend this definition linearly to general
W
.

Definition 3.7

(Jacobi algebra)
.

The Jacobi algebra associated to
(Q,W)
is defined to be

\Jac(Q,W):=\mathbb{C}Q/\langle\partial W/\partial a|a\in Q_{1}\rangle.

Example 1

(Canonical Cubic Potential)
.

Given a quiver
Q
, we consider the quiver
\tilde{Q}
, given by adding loops
\omega_{i}
to each vertex
i\in\overline{Q}_{0}
. Then the tripled quiver
\tilde{Q}
carries the canonical cubic potential

\widetilde{W}=\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\omega_{i}\right)\left(\sum_{a\in Q_{1}}[a,a^{*}]\right).

We have an isomorphism

\mathrm{\mathrm{\Pi}}_{Q}[\omega]\simeq\Jac(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W})

for the polynomial ring
\mathrm{\mathrm{\Pi}}_{Q}[\omega]
where
\omega\mapsto\sum\omega_{i}
. For
Q
not of finite type, the representation category of
\Jac(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W})
is the 3CY completion of the 2CY category of representations of
\mathrm{\mathrm{\Pi}}_{Q}

[
44
]
[Section 5].

Definition 3.8

(Nilpotent Representation)
.

We define the notion of nilpotency as considered in
[
7
]
, which is based on the work of Lusztig
[
54
]
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{\overline{Q}}\subset\mathbb{C}\overline{Q}
-mod be the subcategory of semi-nilpotent representations of the quiver
\overline{Q}
, i.e representations
\rho
for which there exist some
l
such that
\mathbb{C}\overline{Q}_{\geq l}\cdot\rho=0
. Furthermore,

•

We say that a representation of
\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}
is nilpotent if underlining
\mathbb{C}\overline{Q}
module is nilpotent. We shall denote that subcategory of representations by
\mathcal{N}
.

•

Let
\widetilde{Q}
be the tripled quiver introduced in Example
1
. We define
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathbb{C}\widetilde{Q}
-mod to be the subcategory for which underlining
\overline{Q}
module is in
\mathcal{N}_{\overline{Q}}
.

We will also consider representations under stability conditions.

Definition 3.9

(Semistability)
.

Given a quiver
Q
, a stability condition is a tuple
\zeta\in\mathbb{Q}^{Q_{0}}
. For a non-zero representation
\rho
of
Q
, we define the slope

\mu_{\zeta}(\rho):=\frac{\chi_{\zeta}(\dim(\rho)):=\zeta\cdot\dim(\rho)}{|\dim(\rho)|}.

Note that it only depends on
\dim(\rho)
. A representation
\rho
is semistable if for all non-zero, proper
\rho^{\prime}\subset\rho
, we have
\mu_{\zeta}(\rho^{\prime})\leq\mu_{\zeta}(\rho)
and stable if the inequalities are strict. We say that
\zeta
is generic for dimension
\mathbf{d}
if for all
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}<\mathbf{d}
, we have
\mu_{\zeta}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\neq\mu_{\zeta}(\mathbf{d})
.

For
\mu\in(-\infty,\infty)
a slope, we denote by

\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\subset\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}

the submonoid of dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}
such that
\mathbf{d}=0
or
\mu_{\zeta}(\rho)=\mu
.

3.1.
Torus Actions

Given any quiver
Q
and a lattice
N=\mathbb{Z}^{r}
, let the map
\textbf{w}:Q_{1}\rightarrow N
be a weighting function on the edges. Fix
T=\Hom_{\textrm{Group}}(N,\mathbb{C}^{*})
. For every
a\in Q_{1}
, we can define a homomorphism
T\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{*}
given by
t\rightarrow t(\textbf{w}(a))
. This gives a morphism of stacks
\lambda_{a}\colon\textrm{BT}\rightarrow\BC
. Taking pullback, defines a cohomology class
\textbf{t}(a)\in\HH^{2}(\textrm{BT},\mathbb{Q})
given by
\textbf{t}(a)=\lambda_{a}^{*}(u)
where
u
is the generator of
\HH^{2}(\BC,Q)\simeq\mathbb{Q}[u]
. Given a path
p=a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
on the quiver
Q
, we define its weight to be
\textbf{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{i=n}\textbf{w}(a_{i})
. A linear combination of paths is said to be homogeneous if the weights of each path in the linear combination are the same.

4.
Moduli Spaces of quiver representations

Given a quiver
Q
and dimension
\mathbf{d}
, define

\displaystyle\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)

\displaystyle:=\prod_{a\in Q_{1}}\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(s(a))},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(t(a))})

\displaystyle\GL_{\mathbf{d}}

\displaystyle:=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\GL_{\mathbf{d}(i)}

where the group
\GL_{\mathbf{d}}
acts on
\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
by conjugation. Let

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)=\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)/\GL_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)

be the moduli stack of
\mathbf{d}
dimensional representations of
Q
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)]^{\GL_{\mathbf{d}}})
be the coarse moduli space parametrizing semisimple points. We then have the affinization map given by taking the direct sum of successive quotients in the Jordan-Holder filtration of the representation

\JH_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q).

There is an action of
T
on
\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
by

(t,(\rho_{a})_{a\in Q_{1}})\rightarrow(t(\textbf{w}(a))\cdot\rho_{a})_{a\in Q_{1}}

and then we define

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T}(Q):=\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)/(\GL_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\times T).

Similarly, we define the stack

\mathcal{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q):=\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)/T

where
T
acts on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
since the action of
\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
commutes with the action of the gauge group
\GL_{\mathbf{d}}
. We have an induced morphism

\JH^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q).

We can also consider the versions of these objects with stability conditions. Given a King’s stability condition
\zeta
, we define
\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)\subset\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
to be the open subvariety of
\zeta
semistable representations. We refer
[
77
]
for an excellent introduction to quiver moduli spaces.

We define the quotient stack
\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q):=\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)/(\GL_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\times T)
. When there is no
T
, we have the coarse moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
given by GIT Quotient. Since the points of this variety are in bijection with polystable representations, it is easy to see that
T
acts on
\mathcal{M}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
. We define
\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
to be the quotient stack
\mathcal{M}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)/T
. We then have the induced morphism

\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q).

We denote by
\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}=\oplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}:\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
, where
\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}:=\oplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
and
\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}:=\oplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
. Finally for two dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
, we define
\Rep^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Rep^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
to be the subvariety of representations such that the flag
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
is preserved. Let
\GL_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\times T\subset\GL_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\times T
be the subgroup preserving this flag. We then define the quotient stack of extensions

\mathrm{Ext}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}(Q):=\Rep^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}(Q)/(\GL_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\times T).

For any algebra
A
given by the quotient of
\mathbb{C}Q
by a two sided ideal which is preserved by
T
, we define quotient stack
\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(A)
as the
\GL_{\mathbf{d}}
quotient of
\zeta
semistable
\mathbf{d}
dimensional representations of
A
. We similarly have stacks
\Ext^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}(Q)
and coarse moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(A)
. We will be focusing especially on the case when
A=\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}
, which is the quotient of the path algebra of the doubled quiver
\overline{Q}
. This stack can be more conceptually understood in the following way.

4.1.
Representations of preprojective algebra

Let
\overline{Q}
be the doubled quiver. Then we can identify

\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbf{d}}(\overline{Q})\simeq T^{*}\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)

by the pairing between
\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(s(a))},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(t(a))})
and
\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(t(a))},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}(s(a))})
for any arrow
a\in Q_{1}
. The symplectic manifold
T^{*}\mathrm{Rep}_{d}(Q)
carries the action of Gauge group
\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
with moment map

\mu_{Q,\mathbf{d}}:\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(\overline{Q})\rightarrow\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}}:=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}_{i}}

given by

(\rho(a),\rho(a^{*}))_{a\in Q_{1}}\mapsto\sum_{a\in Q_{1}}[\rho(a),\rho(a^{*})].

Here we have identified
\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}}
with
\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\vee}
by the trace pairing. Then a substack of
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\overline{Q})
, the quotient stack
\mu^{-1}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(0)/\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
can be identified with the stack of representations of the preprojective algebra
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})=\mu^{-1,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(0)/\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
be the open substack of
\zeta
semisstable stable quiver representations. We may also consider GIT quotients
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})
. Similar to quiver representations, we have semisimplification morphism

\JH:\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi_{Q}})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi_{Q}}).

When the torus
T
preserves the moment map relations, we can construct moduli spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\mathrm{\Pi_{Q}})
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\mathrm{\Pi_{Q}})
in exactly similar way as before.

Earlier, we defined the notion of nilpotency
\mathcal{N}
for preprojective algebra representation. Following Lusztig, we can define Lusztig nilpotent stack
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q})
as the closed substack
\mu_{\mathbf{d}}^{-1,\mathcal{N}}(0)/GL_{\mathbf{d}}
of nilpotent representations of
\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}
where
\mu_{\mathbf{d}}^{-1,\mathcal{N}}(0):=(\mu^{-1}_{\mathbf{d}}(0)\cap\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{N}}(\overline{Q})
.

5.
Vanishing Cycles

Vanishing cycles provide a coefficient system of counting points in the moduli space of objects in 3CY categories, which are locally a critical locus. In this section, we will introduce vanishing cycles and the properties we need in later sections. For more details, refer to
[
43
]
,
[
15
]
and
[
1
]
. Let
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a function on a smooth complex variety
X
. Then we consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{>0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{z=0\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathfrak{Re}(z)\leq 0\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathfrak{Re}(z)>0\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\leq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

Definition 5.1

(Nearby cycle functor)
.

The perverse
5
5

5

We have shifted the usual functor by [-1].
nearby cycle functor

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{\Psi}_{f}:\mathrm{D}^{+}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{+}(X)

is defined to be

\mathrm{\Psi}_{f}:=i_{*}i^{*}r_{*}r^{*}[-1]

By adjunction, there is a natural transformation
\mathrm{id}\rightarrow r_{*}r^{*}
, precomposing with
i_{*}i^{*}
yields a natural transformation

(6)

i_{*}i^{*}[-1]\rightarrow{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{\Psi}_{f}.

Definition 5.2

(Vanishing cycle functor)
.

The vansishing cycle functor

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{\Phi}_{f}:\mathrm{D}^{+}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{+}(X)

is defined to be the cone of above morphism

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{\Phi}_{f}:=\mathrm{Cone}(i_{*}i^{*}[-1]\rightarrow{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{\Psi}_{f})

These functors behave nicely with the perverse structure, in particular, the
vanishing cycle sheaf

:\pPhi_{f}(\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{X})
is a perverse sheaf on
X
.

Like perverse sheaves, vanishing cycles can be generalized to Artin stacks (See
[
51
]
and
[
90
]
. In this thesis, we will mainly be dealing with the case of smooth quotient stacks
X/G
, where one can understand its cohomology as the equivariant cohomology
H_{G}(X,\pPhi_{f})
.

Definition 5.3

(Critical cohomology)
.

Given a function
f:\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we define critical cohomology with slight abuse of notation:

\HH(\mathfrak{X},\pPhi_{f}):=\HH(\mathfrak{X},\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{X}}).

The vanishing cycle functor enjoys many nice properties, which we shall explain now. We refer
[
15
]
for more details. We assume that all the stacks considered are quotient stacks.

5.1.
Thom-Sebastiani

There is an analogue of the Künneth isomorphism for the critical cohomology. Let
f
and
g
be two functions on smooth stacks
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
respectively. The Thom-Sebastiani theorem states that there is a natural equivalence

\TS:\pPhi_{f\boxplus g}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})|_{f^{-1}(0)\times g^{-1}(0)}\simeq\pPhi_{f}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\pPhi_{g}\mathcal{F}

and when
\Supp(\pPhi_{f\boxplus g}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}))\subset f^{-1}(0)\times g^{-1}(0)
, we get an isomorphism on cohomology

\TS\colon\HH(\mathfrak{X},\pPhi_{f}\mathcal{F})\otimes\HH(\mathfrak{Y},\pPhi_{f}\mathcal{G})\simeq\HH(\mathfrak{X}\times\mathfrak{Y},\pPhi_{f\boxplus g}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G})).

5.2.
Pullback

Given a morphism
p\colon\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}
between smooth stacks, there is a natural morphism

p^{*}\pPhi_{f}\rightarrow\pPhi_{fp}p^{*}

which is an isomorphism when the map
p
is smooth. Applying this to
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
and pushing forward to the point, we get a pullback map

p^{*}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{Y},\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}})\rightarrow\HH(\mathfrak{X},\pPhi_{fp}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}}).

5.3.
Verdier Duality

There is a natural isomorphism
\mathbb{D}\pPhi_{f}\simeq\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{D}
where
\mathbb{D}
is the Verdier duality functor.

5.4.
Pushforward

Given any morphism
p\colon\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}
and a function
f\colon\mathfrak{Y}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
there is a natural map

\pPhi_{f}p_{*}\rightarrow p_{*}\pPhi_{fp}

which is an isomorphism when
p
is proper. Applying this to
\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}}
and composing gives a morphism
\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}\rightarrow p_{*}(\pPhi_{fp}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}})
. Applying
\mathbb{D}
and pushing forward to the point yields a map for
p
proper:

p_{*}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{X},\pPhi_{fp}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}})\rightarrow\HH(\mathfrak{Y},\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})[2(\dim(\mathfrak{Y})-\dim(\mathfrak{X}))].

5.5.
Dimension Reduction

Let
X=X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}
be a smooth variety, let
f
be a function on
X
of weight one for the scaling action on
\mathbb{A}^{n}
and trivial action on
X
and let
\pi\colon X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be the projection map so that we can write

f=\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}x_{i}\pi^{*}(f_{i})

where
f_{i}
are functions on
X^{\prime}
and
x_{i}
are coordinates on
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Let

Z^{\prime}:=\{x\in X^{\prime}\mid f_{i}(x)=0\ \forall\ x\in X^{\prime}\}

and let
Z:=\pi^{-1}(Z^{\prime})
. Let
i_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
, and
i:Z\rightarrow X
be the inclusions. Then the natural map

\pi_{!}\pPhi_{f}\pi^{*}\rightarrow\pi_{!}\pi^{*}(i_{Z^{\prime}})_{*}(i_{Z^{\prime}})^{*}

is an isomorphism
[
17
]
[Appendix A].

Example 2
.

We apply dimension reduction isomorphism to the sheaf
\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{Q}_{X^{\prime}}
. This gives an isomorphism of complexes

\pi_{!}\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{X}\simeq\pi_{!}\pi^{*}(i_{Z^{\prime}})_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}}\simeq\pi_{!}i_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{Z}=\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}}[-2n].

Taking global sections gives an isomorphism of cohomology

(7)

\HH_{c}(X,\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{X})\simeq\HH_{c}(Z,\mathbb{Q}_{Z})\simeq\HH_{c}(Z^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}}[-2n]).

Applying Verdier duality gives an isomorphism of cohomology:

\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(Z^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}}[-2n])\simeq\HH(X,\pPhi_{f}(\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{X})[\dim(X)]).

This isomorphism also extends to quotient stacks. Suppose that an algebraic group
G
acts on
X^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{A}^{n}
such that the function
f
is also
G
invariant. Then we get an isomorphism of sheaves

\pi_{!}\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}_{X/G}\simeq\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}/G}[-2n].

Taking Verdier dual gives an isomorphism of sheaves

(8)

\mathbb{D}\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{\prime}/G}[2n-(\dim(X/G))]\simeq\pi_{*}\pPhi_{f}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{X/G}.

6.
Kontsevich-Soibelman Cohomological Hall algebra

We recall the construction of the cohomological Hall algebra of a quiver with potential from
[
48
]
. Let
Q
be any quiver with potential
W
and let
T
be any torus defined by weighting
\textbf{w}:Q_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{r}
such that the weight of potential
W
is 0. Then given any stability condition
\zeta
and slope
\mu
, we consider the graded vector space

\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}

where

\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}:=\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr^{\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}),

and
\Tr(W)^{\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the trace function
6
6

6

We shall ignore the subscripts from
\Tr(W)^{\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}
when context is clear.
, the grading is given by dimension and cohomology and
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}
is the constant
T
equivariant perverse sheaf defined
7
7

7

We choose this twist since we want to think of
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W}
as version of

with equivariant parameters. For

we choose the sheaf so that restriction to the fibres of projection

are perverse.
by

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}[-(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})].

We then consider the convolution diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q)\times_{[\pt/T]}\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\times\pi_{3}}

By taking the inverse of the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism (

), we have the isomorphism of graded vector spaces:

\displaystyle\textrm{TS}^{-1}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

\displaystyle\otimes\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

\displaystyle\simeq\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}\boxplus\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

, so by taking the pullback (

), we have the morphism of graded vector spaces

\displaystyle j^{*}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),

\displaystyle\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}\boxplus\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

\displaystyle\simeq\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q)\times_{[\pt/T]}\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}\boxplus\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

By taking pullback (

we have the morphism of graded vector spaces

\displaystyle(\pi_{1}\times\pi_{3})^{*}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}(Q)

\displaystyle\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)|_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)})[-(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1})-(\mathbf{d}_{2},\mathbf{d}_{2})].

since
\dim(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q))=-(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})
. Then finally doing the pushforward (

) along
\pi_{2}
, gives a morphism of graded vector spaces

\displaystyle(\pi_{2})_{*}\colon\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)|_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)})[-(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1})-

\displaystyle(\mathbf{d}_{2},\mathbf{d}_{2})]\rightarrow

\displaystyle\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

Note that above respects the cohomological grading since

\dim(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}})-2\dim(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q))=-(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1})-(\mathbf{d}_{2},\mathbf{d}_{2}).

Composing the above morphisms together gives a morphism of cohomologically graded vector spaces

m_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}^{T}:=(\pi_{2})_{*}(\pi_{1}\times\pi_{3})^{*}j^{*}\textrm{TS}^{-1}\colon\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}_{2}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}.

Taking the direct sum of maps
m_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}
over all
(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2})\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}
defines a map of
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded vector spaces

m^{\zeta}_{\mu}\colon\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W\mu}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}

where by
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}
we mean the dimension grading while
\mathbb{Z}
is the cohomological grading. The map
m^{\zeta}_{\mu}
in fact gives
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
a structure of
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded associative algebra structure
[
48
]
[Section 2.3]. We will often denote the multiplication by
\star
. We note that when
\zeta=(0,\cdots,0)
and
\mu=0
then
\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)=\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
and the direct sum is over all
\mathbf{d}
in
\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
. We will denote the resulting algebra by
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W}
and
\mathcal{A}_{Q,W,\mu}
for the case without
T
. Note that by definition
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
is a
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)
linear algebra.

6.1.
Nilpotent 3d CoHA

Following
[
15
]
, one can define a CoHA structure for any geometrically defined Serre subcategory of the quiver representations. In this thesis, we will only consider a case of this construction which is given by the Serre category
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
of nilpotent representations of the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
, defined in Section
3.8
. Let
\overline{\omega}:\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\widetilde{Q})\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(\widetilde{Q})
denote the inclusion of the stacks, where
\mathfrak{M}^{\overline{N}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q})
is the stack of
\zeta
semistable
\mathbf{d}
dimensional representations of quiver
\widetilde{Q}
. We consider the graded vector space

\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{Q,W,\mu}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}

where we define

\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}:=\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),\overline{\omega}_{*}(\overline{\omega})^{!}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

Then, in the same way as the last section, the CoHA convolution diagram constructs an associative algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{Q,W,\mu}
.

6.2.
Relative CoHA

The algebra
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
admits a lift to an algebra object
\mathcal{RA}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
in the derived category
\mathrm{D}^{+}(\Per^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)))
of perverse sheaves on the coarse moduli space.
8
8

8

The shifted perverse
t
structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}_{c}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q))
is defined by setting
{}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\!\tau^{\leq i}:={}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\tau^{\leq i-\dim(T)}
and
{}^{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\!\tau^{\geq i}:={}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\tau^{\geq i-\dim(T)}
. Let
\Per^{{}^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{X}/T)
to be heart with respect to this shifted perverse structure. This is defined so that for any
\mathcal{F}\in\Per^{{}^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{X}/T)
,
(\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}/T)^{*}(\mathcal{F})\in\Per(\mathfrak{X})
.
This lift is sometimes useful in studying this algebra as many of the products in algebra can then be interpreted as morphisms of semisimple Perverse sheaves, which are relatively easier to understand. Let

\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q):=\coprod_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)

and similarly let

\mathcal{M}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q):=\coprod_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\mathcal{M}^{\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q).

We denote
\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
and
\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
by
\mathfrak{M}^{T}(Q)
and
\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
respectively, in the case when
\zeta=0,\mu=0
. Finally let
\JH^{T}_{\mu}:=\coprod_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}}\JH^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}:\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
to be the semisimplification morphism.

We consider the direct sum map

\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)\times\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)\xrightarrow{\oplus}\mathcal{M}^{\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)

It is proved in
[
59
]
that this morphism is a finite and commutative morphism of schemes. This morphism lifts to the
T
quotients

\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)\times_{[\pt/T]}\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)\xrightarrow{\oplus^{T}}\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)

is also finite and commutative. We consider the monoidal product

\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{\oplus^{T}}\mathcal{G}:=\oplus^{T}_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{[\pt/T]}\mathcal{G})

over
[\pt/T]
on
\mathrm{D}^{+}(\Per^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q))
, allowing us to consider algebra objects in
\mathrm{D}^{+}(\Per^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q))
. Since the morphism
\oplus^{T}
is finite, it implies that the monoidal product is bi-exact and symmetric.

Let
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times[\pt/T]
be the stack consisting of the copy of
[\pt/T]
for every dimension vector
\mathbf{d}\in\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}
. Then the
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}
graded algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{Q,W,\mu}
can be understood as the algebra structure on the object
p_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)}\in\mathrm{D}^{+}(\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times[\pt/T])
where
p:\mathfrak{M}^{\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)\rightarrow\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times[\pt/T]
is the canonical morphism and
\mathrm{\Lambda}^{\zeta}_{\mu}\times[\pt/T]
is a monoid over
[\pt/T]
given by the sum of dimension vectors. We now consider something intermediate.

Let

\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}:=(\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mu})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}(\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)}).

Since, after doing semisimplification, non-trivial extensions are the same as the direct sum, the CoHA convolution diagram lifts to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ext}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{d_{1}}(Q)\times_{[\pt/T]}\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{d_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{d_{1}}(Q)\times_{[\pt/T]}\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{d_{2}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}\times\pi_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\JH^{T}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}}\times\JH^{T}_{\mathbf{d}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\JH^{T}_{\mathbf{d}_{1}+\mathbf{d}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus^{T}}

Now, we can do the same game as in the definition of Kontsevich-Soibelman CoHA, but instead we push forward underlying sheaves to
\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mu}(Q)
. It is proved in
[
15
]
and
[
22
]
that this defines a morphism

\mathcal{R}m:\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}\boxtimes_{\oplus^{T}}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}

which makes
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
an algebra object inside the monoidal category
\mathrm{D}^{+}(\Per^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta}_{\mu}(Q)))
. We will often refer to this as the relative CoHA. Taking cohomology of the moprhism
\mathcal{R}m
above, we obtain the usual CoHA
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
.

6.3.
Tautological bundles

Given a family
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathbf{d}
dimensional
\zeta
semistable representations of
\mathbb{C}Q
we can obtain a line bundle by taking
\Det(\mathcal{F})
. This gives a map
\Det\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)\rightarrow\BCu
where
\BCu=\pt/\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
and we use variable
u
to emphasise that
u\in\HH^{2}(\BCu,\mathbb{Q})
is the tautological generator for this
\mathbb{C}^{*}
. Thus, we have a map of stacks

(9)

(\Det\times\id)\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)\rightarrow\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)

which induces a natural map in the constructible category

(10)

\pPhi_{0\boxplus\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)}\rightarrow(\Det\times\id)_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)}

while by
Thom-Sebastiani
\pPhi_{0\boxplus\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)}\simeq\mathbb{Q}_{\BCu}\boxtimes\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{d}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}(Q)}
and so after shifting by
[(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})]
and taking global sections, we get

(\Det\times\id)^{*}\colon\HH(\BCu,\mathbb{Q})\otimes\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}})\rightarrow\HH(\mathfrak{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),\pPhi_{\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}})

This gives an action of
\mathbb{Q}[u]=\HH(\BCu,\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W,\mu}
which we denote by
\cdot
. We will see later that this action acts by derivation.

We may also pushforward the morphism
10
by
\JH^{T,\zeta}_{d}
to induce similar action of
\mathbb{Q}[u]
on
\mathcal{RA}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W\mu}
.

6.4.
Perverse filtration and BPS Lie algebra

6.4.1.
Sign Twist

We slightly twist the multiplication on
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta}_{Q,W}
so that the resulting algebra satisfies the PBW theorem. Given dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
, we define

\displaystyle\tau(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})=\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})+\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})

Then a bilinear form
\psi
on
(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{Q_{0}}
is defined so that we have

\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})+\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime})\equiv\tau(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})\mod 2.

Then
\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\psi}_{Q,W}
is defined as an algebra on the same vector space, but with the multiplication
m^{\psi}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}=(-1)^{\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})}m_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
. Similarly we define
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\psi}_{Q,W,\mu}
to be the algebra object with the same underlying complex of perverse sheaves on
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{A}^{T,\zeta,\psi}_{Q,W,\mu}
but with the modified multiplication
\mathcal{R}m^{\psi}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}=(-1)^{\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})}\mathcal{R}m_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}

For a general situation of quiver
Q
with potential
W
, we fix a choice of
\psi
. For the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
and canonical potential
\widetilde{W}
, we have

\tau_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})\equiv\sum_{\alpha:i\rightarrow j}\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{j}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{j}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i}

and so we choose

\psi(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})=\chi_{Q}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i}-\sum_{\alpha:i\rightarrow j}\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{j}.

when the quiver
Q
is fixed, we will denote this sign twist by
\chi
.

6.5.
Perverse filtration and integrality

We assume that quiver
Q
is symmetric from now on
9
9

9

This is not essential and there are more general results involving
\mu
generic stability conditions in
[
15
]
. The morphism
\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}}
behaves like a proper map
[
15
]
by which we mean that it satisfies an analog of the decompositon theorem, i.e. the direct image
(\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}
splits, that is we have a non-canonical isomorphism in
\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\Per^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}^{T,\zeta-\mathrm{ss}}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)))
:

(11)

(\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{}^{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\!\mathcal{H}^{i}((\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)})[-i].

Furthermore, it is proved in
[
15
]
that
{}^{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\!\mathcal{H}^{i}((\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)})=0
for all
i<1
, so the above splitting starts from
i\geq 1
. For any
i
, applying the natural transformation
{}^{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\!(\tau^{\leq i})\rightarrow\id
, gives a split morphism

{}^{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\!\tau^{\leq i}((\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\rightarrow((\JH^{T,\zeta}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\pPhi_{\Tr(W)}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

Thus pushing forward all the way to the point, gives an injection
