\beta_{(k+n-2)\cdot\delta}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\Im}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W}}
on
V_{K}
isn’t faithful, which is a contradiction to Proposition
14.15
.
∎

Note that as vector spaces
\mathfrak{im}_{K}+\mathfrak{re}_{K}=\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Thus it remains to understand
[\mathfrak{im}_{K},\mathfrak{re}_{K}]
.

Proposition 16.5
.

We have

[\mathfrak{im}_{K},\mathfrak{re}_{K}]\subset\mathfrak{re}_{K}

and is determined by
[\gamma_{\delta}^{(n)},\alpha_{i}^{(m)}]
,
n\geq 0
and
i\in[0,K],m\geq 0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{im}
is generated by
\gamma_{\delta}^{r},r\geq 0
it suffices to show that
[\gamma_{\delta}^{r},\mathfrak{re}]\subset\mathfrak{re}
. But
\mathfrak{re}
is also generated by
e_{i}^{n}
by the Proposition
16.1
so, in fact we need to consider the commutators between
[\gamma_{\delta}^{r},e_{i}^{n}]
. The commutator
[\gamma_{\delta}^{r},f_{i}^{n}]
is an element of dimension
\delta+\delta_{i}
in
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Since
\mathfrak{im}_{K}
only contains elements whose dimension vector is a multiple of
\delta
, the commutator must be in
\mathfrak{re}_{K}
.
∎

We now sieve back everything together. Recall the Lie algebra
\widetilde{W}_{K+1}^{+}
from Definition
15.4
. By Proposition
15.6
,
\widetilde{W}_{K+1}^{+}
is a negatively determined
\mathbf{Heis}
Lie algebra. We consider the Lie subalgebra
\widetilde{W}_{K+1}^{+,\leq 0}\subset\widetilde{W}_{K+1}^{+}
. It is a Lie subalgebra spanned by
T_{0,0}(X)
where
X\in\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{K+1}
,
T_{m+1,0}(X)
where
\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K+1},m\geq 0
and
t_{n,1},t_{n,0}
for all
n\geq 1
. Similarly, by Proposition
10.11
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is a negatively determined
\mathbf{Heis}
Lie algebra. Let
(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})^{\leq 0}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
be the non positive degree subspace of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Clearly it is generated by
\alpha_{i}^{(0)},i\in[0,K]
and
\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(0)},\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)}
for
n\geq 1
.

Theorem 16.6
.

For any
K\geq 1
, the morphism
F:(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\widetilde{W}_{K+1}^{+,\leq 0}
given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0}

\displaystyle\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(-1)^{n}(n)!(K+1)^{n-1}\left((K+1)t_{n,1}-T_{n,0}(H_{K+1})\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}),\forall i\in[1,K]

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1})

extends to an isomorphism of Lie algebras

\hat{F}:\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{W}^{+}_{K+1}.

Proof.

We check that
F
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
10.6
. We first check the compatibility with
\mathbf{Heis}
actions. We have

\displaystyle F(u\cdot\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta})

\displaystyle=F(\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n}(n)!(K+1)^{n-1}\left((K+1)t_{n,1}-T_{n,0}(H_{K+1})\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{t_{0,2}}{2}-\frac{T_{0,1}(H_{K+1})}{K+1},(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0}\right]

\displaystyle=pF(\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta}),

where the last equality is the consequence of the definition of
p
in Proposition
15.6
and similarly
F(\partial\cdot\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)})=nF(\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(0)})=qF(\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)})
by Proposition
15.6
.
We now check that
F
is a morphism of Lie algebras. Since the structure constants between
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)},\gamma^{(0)}_{m\cdot\delta}]
and
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)},\gamma_{m\cdot\delta}^{(1)}]
for
m,n>1
are determined by relations of
[\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\gamma^{(0)}_{m\cdot\delta}]
and
[\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\gamma_{m\cdot\delta}^{(1)}]
, we only consider the latter. Clearly
F([\gamma^{(0)}_{\delta},\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta}])=0=-(-1)^{n+1}[(K+1)t_{1,0},(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0}]=0
. Next, we see that

\displaystyle[F(\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)}),F(\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta})]

\displaystyle=[(-1)((K+1)t_{1,1}-T_{1,0}(H_{K+1})),(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+1}n!(K+1)^{n+1}t_{n+1,0}

\displaystyle=F(\gamma_{(n+1)\cdot\delta}^{(0)})

\displaystyle=F([\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta}])

where the last equality is Equation (
48
). The same argument holds for the relation
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)},\gamma^{(0)}_{\delta}]=\gamma_{(n+1)\cdot\delta}
. On the other hand via Proposition
16.4
we have relation
[\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)}]=(n-1)/(n+1)\gamma_{(n+1)\cdot\delta}^{(1)}
whose compatibility can be checked in the same way. We now consider relations among
\alpha_{i}^{(0)}
and
\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}
. For
i\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle[F(\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)}),

\displaystyle F(\alpha^{(0)}_{i})]=[(-1)((K+1)t_{1,1}-T_{1,0}(H_{K+1})),T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1})]

\displaystyle=T_{1,0}(E_{i,i+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{K-i}}{2}[T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}),T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}),\cdots,T_{0,0}(E_{K,K+1}),T_{0,0}(E_{i-1,i}),\cdots,T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1})]

\displaystyle=F([\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\alpha^{(0)}_{i}])

via Equation
50
. The same argument holds when
n>1
and when
i=0
. In the same way, for
i\neq 0

\displaystyle[F(\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta}),F(\alpha^{(0)}_{i})]

\displaystyle=[(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0},T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1})]

\displaystyle=0=F([\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta},\alpha^{(0)}_{i}])

and for
i=0
, we have

\displaystyle[F(\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta}),F(\alpha^{(0)}_{0})]

\displaystyle=[(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0},T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1})]

\displaystyle=0=F([\gamma^{(0)}_{n\cdot\delta},\alpha^{(0)}_{0}])

where the last equalities are because of Proposition
8.2
. Finally, the relations between
[\alpha_{i}^{(0)},\alpha_{j}^{(0)}]
are also satisfied since by Proposition
8.2
, the Lie algebra generated by
\alpha^{(0)}_{i}
is exactly the positive half
\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{K+1}
and the above map is just the identification of
\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{K+1}
with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{sl}}^{+}_{K+1}
.

By Propositon
10.6
,
F
extends to a map of
\mathbf{Heis}
modules
\hat{F}
. To check if the map extends to the whole Lie algebra, it suffices to check the relations for
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta},\gamma_{m\cdot\delta}^{(2)}]
,
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}^{(1)},\gamma_{m\cdot\delta}^{(1)}]
,
[\gamma_{n\cdot\delta},\alpha_{i}^{(1)}]
. Since by definition, it is a map of
Heis
modules, this boils down to checking the relation for
[\gamma_{\delta}^{(2)},\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}]
. But again it can be checked by definition that

\hat{F}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(2)})=(-1)((K+1)t_{1,2}-2T_{1,1}(H_{K+1}))

and so

\displaystyle[\hat{F}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(2)}),\hat{F}(\gamma_{n\cdot\delta})]

\displaystyle=[(-1)((K+1)t_{1,2}-2T_{1,1}(H_{K+1})),(-1)^{n}(n-1)!(K+1)^{n}t_{n,0}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+1}(n)!(K+1)^{n}((K+1)t_{n+1,1}-T_{n+1,0}(H_{K+1}))

\displaystyle=2/(n+1)F(\gamma_{(n+1)\cdot\delta}^{0})

\displaystyle=\hat{F}([\gamma_{\delta}^{(2)},\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}]).

where the last equality follows from Proposition
16.4
. Thus
\hat{F}
is a map of Lie algebras. By Proposition
15.7
, the map
\hat{F}
is surjective. The map
\hat{F}
preserves cohomological grading and total dimensions. We note by Proposition
8.2
, that

\displaystyle\dim((\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,i}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})_{|\mathbf{d}|})=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{if }i=-2,|\mathbf{d}|\equiv 0\mod{K+1}\\
K+1&\textrm{if }i\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},|\mathbf{d}|>0\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\dim((\widetilde{W}^{+}_{K+1})^{\mathrm{CG}}_{\dim})=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{if }\mathrm{CG}=-2,\dim\equiv 0\mod{K+1}\\
K+1&\textrm{if }\mathrm{CG}\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\dim>0\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

Since they are the same, it follows that the morphism
\hat{F}
is, in fact, an isomorphism of Lie algebras.
∎

16.1.
Application: CoHA of Kleinian surface

We recall
\pi:S_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
is the minimial resolution of the Kleinian singularity
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}
. By
[
41
]
[1.4,3.4], there is an equivalence of derived categories

\mathrm{\Psi}\colon\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\Coh(S_{K}))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}}\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\textrm{Mod})

which restricts to an equivalence

\mathrm{\Psi}\colon\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}(\Coh_{\textrm{ps}}(S_{K}))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\textrm{b}}({\mathrm{\Pi}}_{Q^{K}}\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\textrm{mod})

where
\Coh_{\textrm{ps}}(S_{K})
is the abelian category of compactly supported coherent sheaves on
S_{K}
. This equivalence doesn’t preserve the heart given by the usual bounded derived
t
structures on the derived category. It is known that the abelian subcategory on the left, which is mapped to the representation category of the finite dimensional representations of the preprojective algebra of the cyclic quiver
\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}}
is the abelian category of compactly supported perverse coherent sheaves associated to
\pi\colon S_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}

[
91
]
[3.1].

Recall that for
i\in[1,K]
,
\mathfrak{B}_{i}(\rho_{\text{reg}})
are the irreducible components of the exceptional fiber
\pi^{-1}(0)
(Section
14.1.4
). Let
\omega\in\textrm{Pic}_{\mathbb{Q}}(S_{K}):=\textrm{Pic}(S_{K})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
polarization of
S_{K}
. By polarization, we mean that
\langle\omega,\mathfrak{B}_{i}(\rho_{\text{reg}})\rangle>0
for all
i\in[1,K]
, where
\langle\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt,\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt\rangle
is the intersection pairing on the Picard group of
S_{K}
. The slope of a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\Coh_{\textrm{ps}}(S_{K})
is defined to be

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega}(\mathcal{F}):=\begin{cases}\frac{\chi(F)}{\text{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{F})\cdot\omega}\textrm{ if }\text{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{F})\cdot\omega\neq 0\\
\infty\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

For any
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}_{>}\cup\infty
, let
\Coh^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})
be the classical truncation of the derived stack of
\omega
-semistable properly supported coherent sheaves of
S_{K}
of slope
\mu
. Then in
[
75
]
[2.3.2] and
[
24
]
[3.1], the authors define a cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{\mu}(S_{K})
on the Borel-Moore homology
\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\Coh^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K}),\mathbb{Q})
of the stack
\Coh^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})
. Furthermore, it is shown that under the above derived equivalence, this algebra is isomorphic to the semistable cohomological Hall algebra of the preprojective algebra of the cyclic quiver. More precisely,

Proposition 16.7
.

[
24
]
[Corollary 3.4] Let
\zeta_{i}=\langle\omega,\mathfrak{B}_{i}(\rho_{\textrm{reg}})\rangle
for
1\leq i\leq K
and

\zeta_{0}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\mu}-\sum_{i=1}^{K}\zeta_{i}&,\textrm{if }\mu\neq\infty\\
-\sum_{i=1}^{K}\zeta_{i}&,\textrm{if }\mu=\infty\end{cases}.

The derived equivalence
\mathrm{\Psi}
induces an algebra isomorphism

\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}},0}.

where
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}},0}
is the
\zeta
-semistable, slope
0
preprojective cohomological Hall algebra of the cyclic quiver
Q^{K}
.

In what follows, we will calculate the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q^{K}},0}
, which by the dimension reduction theorem,
[
18
]
[Section 3.2.2] is isomorphic to the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},0}
. We first consider a more general setting. Given any symmetric quiver
Q
, stability condition
\zeta
and slope
\mu
, consider the Lie subalgebra

\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\zeta}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q},\mu}:=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\mu}\backslash\{\textbf{0}\}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}[u]\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}.

We then consider its universal enveloping algebra

\mathcal{C}^{\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mu}:=\bU(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\zeta}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q},\mu})\subset\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}.

The following proposition gives a general way to realize the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mu}
as a subalgebra of
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mu}
explicitly.

Proposition 16.8
.

There is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{C}^{\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mu}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\zeta,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mu}.

Proof.

For every
\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\mu}
, we have open inclusion
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\zeta}(\widetilde{Q})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}(\widetilde{Q})
, which after restriction, induces a map of algebras
(i^{\zeta}_{\mu})^{*}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
such that

(i^{\zeta}_{\mu})^{*}_{|\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}}\colon\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\mu}\backslash\{\textbf{0}\}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\mu}\backslash\{\textbf{0}\}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}

is an isomorphism by Toda’s theorem (Equation
6.4
). Now since the cup product with the tautological bundle commutes with the open inclusion, it follows that the morphism

(i^{\zeta}_{\mu})^{*}_{|}:\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\theta}\backslash\{\textbf{0}\}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}[u]\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{\mu}\backslash\{\textbf{0}\}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\zeta}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}[u]

is an isomorphism of Lie algebras. But then by the PBW theorem (
6.1
) for
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mu}
, it follows that in fact
(i^{\zeta}_{\mu})^{*}
is an isomorphism.
∎

We now consider the algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\zeta,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},0}
. When
\mu=\infty
, the only
\omega
-semistable properly supported coherent sheaves on
S_{K}
are the
0
dimensional sheaves. The cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}(S)
of
0
-dimensional sheaves on any quasi-projective surface
S
is studied and explicitly calculated in
[
60
]
. We calculate this algebra for
S=S_{K}
using Proposition
16.8
. Note that for
\zeta_{i}>0
and
\zeta_{0}=-\sum\zeta_{i}
, having the slope
\mu_{\zeta}(\mathbf{d})=0
implies that
\mathbf{d}_{i}=\mathbf{d}_{0}=n
for some
n\geq 0
. Thus the cohomolgical Hall algebra is the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie subalgebra
\oplus_{n\geq 1}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},n\cdot\delta}[u]\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}^{K}}
. So by Theorem
16.6
, it follows that

Corollary 16.8.1
.

Let
W(S_{K})\subset\widetilde{W}_{K+1}
be the Lie subalgebra on the subspace spanned by
T_{n,a}(H_{i})
where
i\in[1,K],n\geq 1,a\geq 0
and
t_{n,a}
where
n\geq 1,a\geq 0
with the relations

\displaystyle[t_{m,a},t_{n,b}]

\displaystyle=(na-mb)t_{m+n,a+b-1}

\displaystyle[t_{m,a},T_{n,b}(H_{i})]

\displaystyle=(na-mb)T_{m+n,a+b-1}(H_{i})

\displaystyle[T_{m,a}(H_{i}),T_{n,b}(H_{j})]

\displaystyle=T_{m+n,a+b}([H_{i},H_{j}])=0

Then we have an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}(S_{K})\simeq\bU(W(S_{K})).

When
\mu\neq\infty
, then
\mu_{\zeta}(n\cdot\delta)\neq 0
and in particular the Lie subalgebra
\oplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\Lambda^{\zeta}_{0}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},\mathbf{d}}[u]
is always a Lie subalgebra of the real part
\mathfrak{re}_{K}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Given the stability condition
\zeta=\{\zeta_{i}\mid i\in[0,K]\}
, we are interested in representations of slope
0
. We solve for dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}
, where
\mathbf{d}
is a real positive root of the affine Lie algebra
\widetilde{\mathfrak{sl}}_{K+1}
which satisfies the equality

(53)

\displaystyle\mu_{\zeta}(\mathbf{d})=\sum_{i=1}^{K}\zeta_{i}(\mathbf{d}_{i}-\mathbf{d}_{0})+\mathbf{d}_{0}/\mu=0.

Since
\zeta_{i}>0
, any solution would be of type

n\cdot\delta+[K+1-j,i+j)=(\underbrace{n+1,\cdots,n+1}_{\text{$i$ times}},n,\cdots,n,\underbrace{n+1,\cdots,n+1}_{\text{$j$ times}})

for
i>0
. Note that if
\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
satisfies the equation (
53
) then so does
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}
. Since
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W},n\cdot\delta+[K+1-j,i+j)}
is 1 dimensional, we can identify the generator with
T_{n+1,0}(E_{K+2-j,i+1})
when
j>0
and with
T_{n,0}(E_{1,i+1})
when
j=0
.

Definition 16.9
.

Given
\omega
a
\mathbb{Q}\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt
polarization of
S_{K}
and slope
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}_{>}
, let
\zeta_{i}=\langle\omega,\mathfrak{B}_{i}(\rho_{\textrm{reg}})\rangle
for
1\leq i\leq K
and
\zeta_{0}=1/\mu-\sum_{i=1}^{K}\zeta_{i}
. Let
\mathbf{L}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
be the subset of tuples
(n,i,j)
such that
\mathbf{d}=n\cdot\delta+[K+1-j,i+j)
satisfies equation (
53
). Let
W^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})\subset\widetilde{W}_{K+1}
be the Lie algebra spanned by
T_{n+1,a}(E_{K+2-j,i})
for all
(n,i,j)\in\mathbf{L},j>0,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
T_{n,0}(E_{1,i+1})
for all
(n,i,0)\in\mathbf{L},a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
satisfying

[T_{m,a}(X),T_{n,b}(Y)]=T_{m+n,a+b}([X,Y]).

for any
X,Y\in\mathfrak{gl}_{K+1}
.

We then have by Theorem
16.6
, Proposition
16.8
and Proposition
16.7
that

Corollary 16.9.1
.

Let
\omega
be any
\mathbb{Q}
polarization of
S_{K}
and let
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
be any slope. Then there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})\simeq\bU(W^{\omega}_{\mu}(S_{K})).

17.
Affine Yangians and deformed CoHA

We will now consider cohomological Hall algebras
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
in the case when the torus
T
doesn’t preserve the symplectic form, i.e., in the case when the construction of the affinized BPS Lie algebra doesn’t work. In this case, we get a deformation of the universal enveloping algebra
\textbf{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}[u])
, i.e., Yangians. We calculate these algebras by embedding them into cohomological Hall algebras
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q}}
, which carry a description using the Shuffle product.

17.1.
Injection to shuffle algebra

Given any
T
, with
W=0
, the cohomological Hall algebra can be explicitly described in terms of the shuffle product. Since
\Rep_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
is homotopic to a point, we have as a graded vector space

\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HH_{T}(\pt)[x_{i,n}|i\in Q_{0},1\leq n\leq\mathbf{d}_{i}]^{S_{\mathbf{d}}}

where
S_{\mathbf{d}}:=\prod S_{\mathbf{d}_{i}}
is the product of symmetric groups and
S_{\mathbf{d}_{i}}
acts by permuting the variables
x_{i,1},\dots,x_{i,d_{i}}
. Then it is proved in
[
48
]
[Theorem 2.2]) that
20
20

20

Originally this is proved only in the case when
T
is trivial by localization formula, but then the same proof works in the case when there is
T
equivariance.

Proposition 17.1
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}}
and
g\in\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
then the product is given by

(54)

\displaystyle f(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{|Q_{0}|-1,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{|Q_{0}|-1}})\star g(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{|Q_{0}|-1,\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{|Q_{0}|-1}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in{\textsf{Sh}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}}\sigma(f(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{|Q_{0}|-1,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{|Q|_{0}-1}})g(x_{0,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{0}+1},\dots,x_{|Q_{0}|-1,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{|Q_{0}|-1}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{|Q_{0}|-1}})K_{Q}(\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}};\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}})),

where
K_{Q}(\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}};\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}})
is called the shuffle kernel, and it depends on the quiver precisely by

(55)

K_{Q}(\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}};\textbf{x}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}):=\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{a\in Q_{1}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{s(a)}\\
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{t(a)}<n\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{t(a)}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{t(a)}\end{subarray}}(x_{t(a),n}-x_{s(a),m}+\textbf{t}(a))}{\displaystyle\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\\
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}<n\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\end{subarray}}(x_{i,n}-x_{i,m})}

and
{\textsf{Sh}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\subset S_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
is defined to be the subset of permutations
(\sigma_{i})\in S_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
where
i\in Q_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(1)<\cdots<\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i})\textrm{ and }\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}+1)<\cdots<\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i})

This product is often referred to as the shuffle product.

Remark 17.2
.

For tripled cyclic quiver
\widetilde{Q}_{K}
, also incorporating the twist
\chi
in (
6.4.1
) the shuffle kernel can be written as

(56)

K_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}(x_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}};x_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}):=(-1)^{\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{i+1}}\prod_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\\
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{j}<n<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{j}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\overline{\omega}_{i,j}^{\text{CoHA}}(x_{i,m},x_{j,n})

where

(57)

\overline{\omega}_{i,j}^{\text{CoHA}}(z,w)=\left(w-z+t_{1}\right)^{\delta_{j,i+1}}\left(w-z+t_{2}\right)^{\delta_{j,i-1}}\left(\frac{w-z-t_{1}-t_{2}}{w-z}\right)^{\delta_{j,i}}

In
[
5
]
[Section 5.2], a similarly defined algebra structure on the ring of Laurent symmetric functions is considered as an additive shuffle algebra of cyclic type. Let
{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{Q^{K}}=\oplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{K+1}}{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{Q^{K},\mathbf{d}}
be a
\mathbb{N}^{K+1}
graded vector space, where

{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{Q^{K},\mathbf{d}}:=\mathbb{C}(t_{1},t_{2})(x_{i,n}|i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1},1\leq n\leq\mathbf{d}_{i})^{S_{\mathbf{d}}}

This vector space is endowed with a associative algebra product
\star_{\textrm{BT}}
given by

\displaystyle f(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{K,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{K}})\star_{\textrm{BT}}g(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{K,\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{K}}):=

\displaystyle\Sym\left(f(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{K,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{K}})g(x_{0,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{0}+1},\dots,x_{K,\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{K}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{K}})\prod_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq m\leq\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{i}\\
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{j}<n<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{j}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\end{subarray}}\overline{\omega}_{i,j}(x_{i,m},x_{j,n})\right)\right)

where
21
21

21

We do the substitution
\hbar_{1}=-t_{2},\hbar_{2}=t_{1}+t_{2},\hbar_{3}=-t_{1}
.

\overline{\omega}_{i,j}(z,w)=\left(\frac{w-z+t_{1}}{w-z}\right)^{\delta_{j,i+1}}\left(\frac{w-z+t_{2}}{w-z}\right)^{\delta_{j,i-1}}\left(\frac{w-z-t_{1}-t_{2}}{w-z}\right)^{\delta_{j,i}}.

and for a function
f\in\mathbb{C}(t_{1},t_{2})(x_{i,n}|i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1},1\leq n\leq\mathbf{d}_{i})
, it’s symmetrization is defined to be

\Sym(f):=\left(\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{d}_{i}!}\right)\left(\sum_{\sigma\in S_{\mathbf{d}}}\sigma(f(x_{0,1},\dots,x_{K,\mathbf{d}_{K}}))\right).

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\textrm{Loc}}
be the same vector space as
{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{Q^{K}}
but with the algebra product induced by the algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
. Then the morphism

S:\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\textrm{Loc}}\rightarrow{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{Q^{K}}

given by

S(f(x_{\mathbf{d}}))=(-1)^{\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\binom{\mathbf{d}_{i}}{2}+\mathbf{d}_{i}\mathbf{d}_{i+1}}f(x_{\mathbf{d}})\left(\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{d}_{i}!}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq n\leq\mathbf{d}_{i}\\
1\leq m\leq\mathbf{d}_{i+1}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{x_{i,n}-x_{i+1,m}}\right)\right)

is an algebra isomorphism and so in particular
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\textrm{Loc}}\rightarrow{\textsf{Sh}}^{\textrm{BT}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
is an injection of algebras.

17.1.1.
Homomorphism to shuffle algebra

Given any quiver
Q
with potential
W
and a torus
T
preserving the potential
W
, assume that there exist a weighting
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}:Q_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, for which
W
is homogeneous and of strictly positive weight. Let
Q^{\prime}\subset Q
be the subquiver consiting only those edges
a
for which
\textbf{w}^{\prime}(a)=0
. Then there is a homomorphism from the cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}^{T,\chi}_{Q,W}
to
\mathcal{A}^{T,\chi}_{Q}
. Let
i_{\mathbf{d},T}\colon Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q):=\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}^{-1}(0)\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
be the inclusion where
\Tr(W)_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
. By considering the torus action associated with the weighting
\textbf{w}^{\prime}
, one can construct a homotopy between
Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
and
\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q^{\prime})
.
The vanishing cycle functor comes with a natural map

\pPhi_{W}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}\rightarrow(i_{\mathbf{d},T})_{*}(i_{\mathbf{d},T})^{*}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}

which after taking global sections, gives map of graded vector spaces

i_{\mathbf{d},T}:\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,W,\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\HH(Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q),(i_{\mathbf{d}})^{*}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)})\simeq\mathcal{A}^{T}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}.

Here, the second isomorphism is simply because both
\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)
are homotopic to a point. This gives a map of
\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded vector spaces

i^{T}:\mathcal{A}^{T,\psi}_{Q,W}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{T,\psi}_{Q}.

Then it is proved in
[
6
]
[Prop 4.4] that

Proposition 17.3
.

The morphism

i^{T}:\mathcal{A}^{T,\psi}_{Q,W}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{T,\psi}_{Q}.

is a homorphism of
\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded algebras.

We now show that this morphism respects the action of
\mathbf{Heis}
constructed in Proposition
10.1
.

Proposition 17.4
.

The morphism
i^{T}
is a morphism of
\mathbf{Heis}
modules.

Proof.

Since the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\BC_{u}\times Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\det\times\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\det\times\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times i^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}}

commutes, it follows from the naturality of the morphism (
6
) that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pPhi_{0\boxplus W_{\mathbf{d}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T}(Q)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Det\times\id)_{*}\pPhi_{W_{\mathbf{d}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T}(Q)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\id\times i^{T}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\BCu\times Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(i^{T}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}}

commutes. By taking global sections, it follows that the action by
u
commutes. The compatibility with the
\partial_{u}
action follows in the same way since the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\BCu\times\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{T}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\BCu\times Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\act}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\act}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times i^{T}_{\mathbf{d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{d}}^{T}}

commutes as
\Tr(W)(\rho)=0\implies\Tr(W)(\lambda\rho)=0
for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

∎

The morphism
i^{T}
is not, in general, an injection. We can already see this case for tripled cyclic quivers.

17.1.2.
Not injection for small torus

Since
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is non-trivial by Proposition
8.0.1
, it follows that neither of algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}},\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
are commutative. However by the shuffle formula above,
\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
are commutative. This, in particular, means that the algebra morphisms
i
and
i^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
cannot be injective.

17.1.3.
Injection for generic torus

However for the torus
\mathbb{T}
, it is shown in
[
19
]
[Theorem 10.2] and
[
79
]
that
\HH(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{Q^{K}})\pPhi_{\Tr(\widetilde{W^{K}})})
is a torsion free
\HH_{\mathbb{T}\times\GL_{\mathbf{d}}}
module, which in particular implies that

Proposition 17.5
.

The natural morphism

i^{\mathbb{T}}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}

is an injection of
\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded algebras.

By Proposition
7.6
, we have an isomorphism of cohomologically graded vector spaces

\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]

This morphism restricts to an isomorphism of cohomologically graded
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)
modules
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
. Thus for every element
\alpha_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}},i\in[0,K]
and
\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}},n\geq 1
, we have a unique (up to scalar), non-canonical lift
\widetilde{\alpha_{i}}:=\alpha_{i}\otimes 1
and
\widetilde{\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}}:=\gamma_{n\cdot\delta}\otimes 1
, since both
\HH^{-2}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},n\cdot\delta})
and
\HH^{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\delta_{i}})
are
1
dimensional vector spaces. We then have

Proposition 17.6
.

The
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},W^{K}}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}
is generated as a
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
algebra by
u^{k}\cdot\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}
for all
i\in[0,K],k\geq 0
and
u^{k}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

There is an isomorphism of Lie algebras

\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]

by
[
14
]
[Corollary 11.9]. For every
\alpha\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
we set
\widetilde{\alpha}=\alpha\otimes 1\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
via this isomorphism and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(m_{i})}:=u^{m}\cdot\widetilde{\alpha}
. By Theorem
16.6
, we may identify
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
as a Lie subalgebra of
\widetilde{W}_{K+1}
, spanned by
T_{k,0}(X)
where
k\geq 1,X\in\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
or
k=0,X\in\mathfrak{n}_{K}
where
\mathfrak{n}_{K}\subset\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
is the Lie subalgebra generated by upper diagonal matrices and
t_{k,0}
where
k\geq 1
. For any
k\geq 1
, up to a factor of non-zero scalar,
t_{k,0}
can be written as
t_{k,0}=\ad^{k-1}_{t_{1,1}}(t_{1,0})
. Similarly, each of
T_{k,0}(X)
where
k\geq 1,X\in\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
or
k=0,X\in\mathfrak{n}_{K}
can be written as succesive Lie brackets on
T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}),i\in[1,K]
and
T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1})
. Thus every basis element
\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mathbf{d}}
can be written as a linear combination of products of
\alpha^{(0)}_{0},\alpha^{(0)}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{(0)}_{K},\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)},\gamma_{\delta}^{(0)}
, i.e. there exists an expression
g_{\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}}
such that

g_{\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}}(\alpha^{(0)}_{0},\alpha^{(0)}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{(0)}_{K},\gamma_{\delta}^{(0)},\gamma_{\delta}^{(1)})=\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}.

We fix such an expression for every basis element
\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mathbf{d}}
. We then define a map of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
modules

\psi_{\mathbf{d}}:\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mathbf{d}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[u]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

given by

\psi_{\mathbf{d}}(\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}\otimes f(t_{1},t_{2})\otimes u^{m})=f(t_{1},t_{2})u^{m}\cdot g_{\alpha_{\mathbf{d}}}(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{0},\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{K},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)})

and thus we have map of free
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
modules

\Psi:\Sym\left(\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}}\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\mathbf{d}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[u]\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

However,
\Psi\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]}\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]/(t_{1},t_{2})
is an isomorphism by the PBW theorem (Theorem
6.1
) and thus
\Psi
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
modules by the graded Nakayama lemma and so the statement follows.

∎

17.1.4.
Image in the Shuffle algebra

We now determine the image of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
under the map
i^{\mathbb{T}}
. By Proposition
17.6
, it suffices to understand the image of
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(k)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(k)}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proposition 17.7
.

The image
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})\subset\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is the
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
subalgebra generated by
x_{i,1}^{r}\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\delta_{i}}
for all
r\geq 0,i\in[0,\cdots,K]
and
(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}(x_{0,1}+x_{1,1}+\cdots+x_{K,1})^{r}\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\delta}
for all
r\geq 0
. We have
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(r)})=x_{i,1}^{r}
and
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(r)})=\lambda(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}(x_{0,1}+x_{1,1}+\cdots+x_{K,1})^{r}
for some non-zero scalar
\lambda
.

Proof.

Since
i^{\mathbb{T}}_{\delta_{i}}:\CoHA^{T}_{(0,\cdots,1,\cdots,0)}\simeq\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}][x_{i,1}]
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}=(i^{\mathbb{T}})^{-1}(1)
, Proposition
17.4
implies that

i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(k)})=x_{i,1}^{k}

for all
k\geq 0
. Let
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0})=f
where
f\in\mathbb{Q}[t_{1},t_{2}][x_{0,1},x_{1,1},x_{2,1},\cdots,x_{K,1}]
. Then by the shuffle formula (
54
), it follows that

(58)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0}\star\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}

\displaystyle=f(x_{0,1},x_{1,1},\cdots,x_{i,1},\cdots,x_{K,1})\frac{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1}-t_{1}-t_{2})(x_{i,2}-x_{i-1,1}+t_{1})(x_{i,2}-x_{i+1,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1})}+

\displaystyle f(x_{0,1},x_{1,1},\cdots,x_{i,2},\cdots,x_{K,1})\frac{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2}-t_{1}-t_{2})(x_{i,1}-x_{i-1,1}+t_{1})(x_{i,1}-x_{i+1,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2})}

and

(59)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\star\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0}

\displaystyle=f(x_{0,1},\cdots,x_{i,2},\cdots,x_{K,1})\frac{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1}-t_{1}-t_{2})(x_{i+1,1}-x_{i,1}+t_{1})(x_{i-1,1}-x_{i,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1})}+

\displaystyle f(x_{0,1},\cdots,x_{i,1},\cdots,x_{K,1})\frac{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2}-t_{1}-t_{2})(x_{i+1,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{1})(x_{i-1,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2})}

Since
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)}
is of cohomological degree
-2
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(0)}
is of cohomological degree
0
, the Lie bracket
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}]=0
is of cohomological degree
-2
. Since
\HH^{-2}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\delta+\delta_{i}})=0
, we must have
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}]=0
. Since

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1}+t_{3})(x_{i,2}-x_{i-1,1}+t_{1})(x_{i,2}-x_{i+1,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1})}-

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{3})(x_{i+1,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{1})(x_{i-1,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2})}=

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1}+t_{3})(x_{i+1,1}-x_{i,1}+t_{1})(x_{i-1,1}-x_{i,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,2}-x_{i,1})}-

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2}+t_{3})(x_{i,1}-x_{i-1,1}+t_{1})(x_{i,1}-x_{i+1,1}+t_{2})}{(x_{i,1}-x_{i,2})},

equation (
58
), (
59
) and the condition that
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\star\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0}=\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0}\star\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}
implies that for all
i\in\{0,1,\cdots,K\}
we must have

\displaystyle f(x_{0,1},\cdots,x_{i-1,1},x_{i,1},x_{i+1,1},\cdots,x_{K,1})=f(x_{0,1},\cdots,x_{i-1,1},x_{i,2},x_{i+1,1},\cdots,x_{K,1})

This means that for any
i
,
f
is independent of the variable
x_{i,1}
, i.e.
f\in\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
. Since
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)}
is of cohomological degree
-2
, this implies that the degree of polynomial
f(t_{1},t_{2})
must be
K
. Now
[\gamma_{\delta}^{0},\alpha_{i}^{(1)}]
is an element of cohomological degree
0
in the dimension vector
\delta_{i}+\delta
. Since the
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
action only increases the cohomological degree, it means that up to a scalar, the only non-zero term of cohomological degree
0
in dimension
\delta_{i}+\delta
in
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is
[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{K},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}]
(Proposition
8.2
). Again by the shuffle product description of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
, we can explictly calculate
[i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{K}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{0})]
. We see that for some non-zero constant
\lambda_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}
we have

(60)

[i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{K}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{0})]=\lambda_{i}\frac{(t_{0}+t_{1})^{K}[i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(1)})]}{f(t_{1},t_{2})}

Now Proposition
16.3
implies that there exist some
\mu_{i}\neq 0
such that in
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
,

[\widetilde{\gamma_{\delta}}^{(0)},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(1)}]=\mu_{i}[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{K},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}]+(t_{1},t_{2})i(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}).

This implies

\left(\frac{f(t_{1},t_{2})}{\lambda_{i}(t_{0}+t_{1})^{K}}-\mu_{i}\right)[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{K},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}]\in(t_{1},t_{2})i(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}).

By the PBW theorem (Proposition
6.1
), the only element of cohomological degree
-2
in dimension vector
\delta+\delta_{i}
could be
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)}\star\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(0)}
. We again apply the shuffle product formula to calculate
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)})\star i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(0)})
. We see that

[i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{K}),i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1}),\cdots,i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{0})]\notin(t_{1},t_{2})\cdot i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(0)})\star i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(0)})

and thus
[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{K},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}]\notin(t_{1},t_{2})i(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})
. But
[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{K},\widetilde{\alpha}_{i-1},\cdots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}]\neq 0
by the calculation of
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
in Proposition
8.2
. Thus, we must have

f(t_{1},t_{2})=\lambda_{i}\mu_{i}(t_{0}+t_{1})^{K}.

So
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(0)})=\lambda(t_{0}+t_{1})^{K}
up to a non-zero scalar
\lambda
. Then by Proposition
17.4
,
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(r)})=\lambda(t_{0}+t_{1})^{K}(x_{01}+x_{11}+\cdots+x_{K,1})^{r}
and we are done.

∎

This already allows us to identify a commutative subalgebra inside the image
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})\subset\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
.

Corollary 17.7.1
.

Let
e=\sum_{i\in[0,K]}x_{i,1}\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\delta}
. For each
n\geq 1
, we define

L_{n}:=[\underbrace{e,[e,[\cdots[e,}_{n-1\text{ times }},1]]]]\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},n\cdot\delta}

Then, the subalgebra generated by
L_{n},n\geq 1
is a polynomial subalgebra isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}[L_{1},L_{2},\cdots]
.

Proof.

Consider the Lie subalgebra generated by
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta},n\geq 1
. Then, by Proposition
8.2
, it is a trivial Lie algebra, and the subalgebra generated by this Lie algebra is a polynomial subalgebra inside
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Let us call this algebra
A^{K}
. We show that the image of this under
i^{\mathbb{T}}
up to a factor of polynomials in
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
is exactly the subalgebra generated by the above generators. For any
n\geq 1
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}\in\HH^{-2}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},n\cdot\delta})
consider the commutator
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}]
. Then since
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)}\in\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 3}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}\in\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1}
, it follows that
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}]\in\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 3}
where we recall that
\mathfrak{P}^{\leq i}
denote the perverse filtration defined in Section
6.5
. Thus, it should be a linear combination of elements of the form
\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1},\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1}\star\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1}
or
u\cdot\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1}
. However minimal cohomological degree of
u\cdot\mathfrak{P}^{\leq 1}
is
0
and thus it follows that we must have

(61)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}]=\lambda\widetilde{\gamma}_{(n+1)\cdot\delta}+\sum_{n_{i}+n_{j}=n+1}\lambda_{n_{i},n_{j}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{n_{i}\cdot\delta}\star\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta}+\sum_{k_{i}+k_{j}=n+1}f_{k_{i},k_{j}}(t_{1},t_{2})\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{i}\cdot\delta}\star\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}\cdot\delta}

where
\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta}\in\mathfrak{n}^{-}_{Q^{K}}\subset\HH^{0}(\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},n_{j}\cdot\delta})
for some
\lambda,\lambda_{n_{i},n_{j}}\in\mathbb{C}
and linear functions
f_{k_{i},k_{j}}(t_{1},t_{2})
. We claim that each of
\lambda_{n_{i},n_{j}}=0
. For any
\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta}
, by Proposition
8.2
, there exist
\widetilde{\alpha}_{j}
in dimension
\delta_{j}
such that
[\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta},\widetilde{\alpha}_{j}]\neq 0
. But then since
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}
commutes with
\mathfrak{n}^{-}_{Q^{K}}
, applying
[-,\widetilde{\alpha}_{j}]
to the above equation (
61
) gives that

0=\sum_{n_{i}+n_{j}=n+1}\lambda_{n_{i},n_{j}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{n_{i}\cdot\delta}\star[\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta},\widetilde{\alpha}_{j}].

But each of non-zero
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n_{i}\cdot\delta}\star[\widetilde{\alpha}_{n_{j}\cdot\delta},\widetilde{\alpha}_{j}]
can be extended to a basis of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
by the PBW theorem (Proposition
6.1
). Since the above equation gives a relation, it implies that
\lambda_{n_{i},n_{j}}=0
. Furthemore
\lambda\neq 0
by Proposition
16.2
. Thus it follows that we must have
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}]\in A^{K}
. By equation
61
, it follows that the algebra generated by
[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}],n\geq 1
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}
contains all the generators
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n\cdot\delta}
of
A^{K}
. But then, so does

(62)

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{n}:=[\underbrace{\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)},[\cdots[\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(1)}}_{n-1\text{ times }},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}]]]]\in A^{K}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

Thus algebra generated by
\widetilde{L}_{n},n\geq 1
is
A^{K}
, the commutative subalgebra. Thus, we are done from Proposition
17.7
.

∎

Proposition
17.7
also gives a slight refinement of a particular case of
[
80
]
and
[
72
]
about spherical generation of localized cohomological Hall algebra. We note it down as the argument will be helpful later.

Corollary 17.7.2
.

The
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
linear algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is not a spherically generated
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
algebra, while the localized algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}^{-1}]
is spherically generated.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is not spherically generated follows from the fact that the cohomological degree of
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(r)}\geq 0
, while cohomological degree of
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{0}=-2
. Since the product structure preserves cohomological grading, it is not possible to reach this element by taking products of
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(r)}
, nor by multiplication with any element of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
, since that would also increase cohomological degree.

On the other hand by Proposition
17.7
, up to a factor of non-zero scalar,
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(0)})=(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}},\delta}
. By the argument above
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\gamma_{\delta}^{(0)})
cannot be written as the
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt
linear combination of products of
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(r)})
. However, we can write it as a
\mathbb{C}[t_{1}^{\pm 1},t_{2}^{\pm 1}]\penalty-\penalty\hskip 0.0pt
linear combination of the products of
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(r)})
. Let

(63)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{0}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{1}+\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{1}\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{0}

(64)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle=[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{0},\widetilde{\alpha}^{(1)}_{1}]+[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{(1)}_{0}]

for
K=1
and

(65)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{K+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(0)}\cdot\left([\widetilde{\alpha}_{i+1}^{(0)},[\widetilde{\alpha}_{i+2}^{(0)},\cdots,[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i+K-1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i+K}]]]\right)

(66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{K+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i},[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(1)}_{i+1},[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i+2},[\cdots,[\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i+K-1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{(0)}_{i+K}]]]]

for
K\geq 2
.

Note that second term in
\mathcal{Z}_{K+1}
is
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(1)}_{i+1}
. Using the shuffle product formula (
54
), it follows that for

i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Y}_{2})=(-2)((x_{1,1}-x_{0,1})^{2}+t_{1}t_{2})

and

i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Y}_{K+1})=(-1)^{K}(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K-1}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\left(x_{i+1,1}x_{i-1,1}-2x_{i+1,1}x_{i,1}+x_{i,1}^{2}+t_{1}t_{2}\right)

for
K\geq 2
. Similarly, we calculate that

i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Z}_{2})=(-2)(t_{1}+t_{2})(x_{1,1}-x_{0,1})^{2}

and

i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Z}_{K+1})=(-1)^{K}(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{K+1}}\left(x_{i+1,1}x_{i-1,1}-2x_{i+1,1}x_{i,1}+x_{i,1}^{2}\right)

for
K\geq 2
.

Thus for all
K\geq 1
, it follows that

(t_{1}+t_{2})i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Y}_{K+1})-i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Z}_{K+1})=(-1)^{K}(K+1)(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}(t_{1}t_{2})

and thus it follows that

\frac{1}{(-1)^{K}(K+1)}\left(\left(\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{t_{1}t_{2}}\right)i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Y}_{K+1})-\left(\frac{1}{t_{1}t_{2}}\right)i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{Z}_{K+1})\right)=(t_{1}+t_{2})^{K}

Thus by Proposition
17.4
, it follows that for any
K\geq 1
, we can write
\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{r}
as a non-zero scalar multiple of

(67)

\mathcal{K}^{r}_{K+1}:=u^{r}\cdot(\frac{(t_{1}+t_{2})\mathcal{Y}_{K+1}-\mathcal{Z}_{K+1}}{t_{1}t_{2}})

∎

Now that we know the image of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
in the shuffle algebra, we show that the image is an integral form of an affine Yangian.

17.2.
Affine Yangian

Let
n\geq 2
. Let
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
be the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{n}
considered in
[
5
]
, which is a slight reparametrization of the affine Yangian defined by
[
33
]
. The definition of the affine Yangian for
n=2
was first given in
[
47
]
and
[
5
]
. Recall
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
from the Definition
2.2
, which is slight modification of the affine Yangian.

Definition 17.8
.

For any
n\geq 2
, let the positive half
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
of the affine Yangian
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
to be the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
sub algebra of
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
generated by the elements
X^{+}_{i,r}
where
i=0,\dots,n-1
.

Note that we can give a
dimension
grading to the algebra
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
by declaring
\textbf{dim}(X^{+}_{i,r})=\delta_{i}
where
\delta_{i}=(0,\cdots,1,\cdots,0)\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
. We also have a
cohomological
grading given by declaring
\CG(X^{+}_{i,r})=2r
and
\CG(\hbar_{1})=\CG(\hbar_{2})=2
. Then the size of this algebra is known due to earlier work. We have

Proposition 17.9
.

There is an isomorphism of free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
modules

\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))\simeq\Sym(\mathcal{L}_{n})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]

where
\mathcal{L}_{n}
is
\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded vector space of graded dimension

(68)

\displaystyle\dim((\mathcal{L}_{n})^{\CG}_{\mathbf{dim}})=\begin{cases}n-1&\textrm{if }\CG=0,\mathbf{dim}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\cdot\delta\\
n&\textrm{if }\CG\geq 1,\mathbf{dim}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\cdot\delta\\
1&\textrm{if }\CG\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\mathbf{dim}\text{ is a positive real root of }\widetilde{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n}\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

Proof.

We define a filtration
F
of
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
by assigning
X^{+}_{i,r}
degree
2r
and rest
0
. Then the associated graded algebra
\mathrm{Gr}_{F}(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n)))\simeq\mathbf{U}(\mathcal{L}_{n})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
where
\mathcal{L}
is Lie algebra generated by relations
\mathcal{L}_{n}
is a Lie algebra over
\mathbb{C}
, generated by
X^{+}_{i,r},r\geq 0,i\in[0,n-1]
with the defining relations

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r+1},X^{+}_{i,s}]-[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i,s+1}]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r+2},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]-2[X^{+}_{i,r+1},X^{+}_{i+1,s+1}]+[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i+1,s+2}]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}_{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}}[X^{+}_{i,r_{1}},[X^{+}_{i,r_{2}},[X^{+}_{i,r_{3}},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]]]

\displaystyle=0

when
n=2

and

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{+},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]

\displaystyle=[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i+1,s+1}]

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{+},X^{+}_{i-1,s}]

\displaystyle=[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i-1,s+1}]

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{+},X^{+}_{i,s}]

\displaystyle=[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i,s+1}]

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=0\ \forall|i-j|>1

\displaystyle\Sym_{r_{1},r_{2}}[X^{+}_{i,r_{1}},[X^{+}_{i,r_{2}},X^{+}_{i\pm 1,s}]]

\displaystyle=0

when
n>2
.

Given any commutative algebra
A
, we can consider the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(A)
, defined by the Lie bracket
[X\otimes a,Y\otimes b]=[X,Y]\otimes ab
where
X,Y\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
and
a,b\in A
are arbitrary elements. Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}(A)
be the universal central extension of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(A)
which is as vector spaces
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(A)\oplus\mathrm{\Omega}^{1}(A)/dA
, where
\mathrm{\Omega}^{1}(A)/dA
is the space of
1
forms on the affine variety
\Spec(A)
, modulo the exact forms (We refer to
[
61
]
and
[
33
]
for details). The Lie algebra is defined by

[z_{1}\otimes a_{1},z_{2}\otimes a_{2}]=[z_{1},z_{2}]\otimes a_{1}\cdot a_{2}+\langle z_{1},z_{2}\rangle a_{2}da_{1}

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the Killing form.
We consider the setting when
A=\mathbb{C}[u^{\pm 1},v]
. Let
\mathfrak{b}^{+}_{n}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}[u^{\pm 1},v]
be the positive half. It can be identified as the Lie algebra spanned by
Xu^{a}v^{b}
where
X\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n},a\geq 1,b\geq 0
and
Xv^{b}
where
X\in\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{n}
and
K_{b}[a]
where
a,b\geq 1
, such that they satisfy the relations

[Xu^{m_{1}}v^{n_{1}},Yu^{m_{2}}v^{n_{2}}]=[X,Y]\otimes u^{m_{1}+m_{2}}v^{n_{1}+n_{2}}+\langle X,Y\rangle(m_{2}n_{1}-m_{1}n_{2})K_{m_{1}+m_{2}}[n_{1}+n_{2}],

with
K_{b}[a]
central.

For
n>2
, by
[
33
]
[Lemma 3.4] and for
n=2
, by
[
23
]
[Proposition IV.3.10]; we have an isomoprhism of Lie algebras
\mathcal{L}_{n}\simeq\mathfrak{b}^{+}_{n}
given by
X^{+}_{i,r}\mapsto E_{i,i+1}v^{r}
and
X^{+}_{0,r}\mapsto E_{n,1}uv^{r}
. The advantage is that now we have an explicit spanning set of the Lie algebra
\mathcal{L}_{n}
. The
\dim
and
\CG
grading makes
\mathcal{L}_{n}
a
\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded vector spaces. Computing these graded dimensions proves the above theorem.
∎

Proposition 17.10
.

Assume
K\geq 1
. Then we have a homomorphism of algebras
22
22

22

A 1-parameter version of this proposition is also proved in
[
93
]
for any loop-free quiver
Q
.

\mathrm{\Phi}:\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

given by the assignment
X^{+}_{i,s}\mapsto\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(s)}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
17.5
, there is an injection of algebras
i^{\mathbb{T}}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
.
Since
i^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathrm{\Phi}(X^{+}_{i,s}))=x^{s}_{i,1}
. We can use the explicit shuffle product (Equation
55
) on
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}}}
to check that the defining relations (
17.8
) of the positive half of the affine Yangian are satisfied. We refer author’s thesis
[
36
]
for details.
∎

We now show that in fact the morphism
\mathrm{\Phi}
is an injection
23
23

23

In an earlier version of the paper, for
K>1
, we proved this by comparing graded dimension after taking Perverse filtration.
.

Proposition 17.11
.

Assume
K\geq 1
. Then we have an injection of algebras:

\mathrm{\Phi}:\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

given by the assignment
X^{+}_{i,s}\mapsto\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(s)}
.

Proof.

We show that, in fact, we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{\Phi}:\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

Clearly by definition,
\mathrm{\Phi}
is a surjection on its image. The spherical subalgebra
\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is cohomologically and dimensionally graded. It is shown in
[
81
]
[Theorem 5.18] that
\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is isomorphic to the nilpotent cohomological Hall algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\mathcal{N},\chi}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
. We now compute the graded dimension of nilpotent CoHA. By Theorem
7.8
, we have an isomorphism of free
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
modules

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}].

In Example
26
, we proved that there is an isomorphism of graded vector spaces:

\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\simeq\Sym(\HH(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{N}_{\overline{Q}}}(\overline{Q}),i^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}})[u]).

and

\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}\dim(\HH^{i}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{N}_{\overline{Q}}}(\overline{Q}),i^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}}))q^{i/2}=a_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q).

and thus by calculation of Kac polynomial for cyclic quiver in Equation
8.1
, it follows that

(69)

\displaystyle\dim(\HH(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathcal{N}_{\overline{Q}}}(\overline{Q}),i^{!}\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathrm{\Pi}_{Q}}[u])^{i})=\begin{cases}K&\textrm{if }i=0,\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\cdot\delta\\
K+1&\textrm{if }i\geq 1,\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\cdot\delta\\
1&\textrm{if }i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\mathbf{d}\text{ is a positive real root of }\widetilde{\mathfrak{sl}}_{K+1}\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

which matches the graded dimensions of the affine Yangian in Proposition
17.9
. Thus, the morphism
\mathrm{\Phi}
is an injection.
∎

Remark 17.12
.

From the above argument, it follows that there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
linear algebras
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. This statement is now also proved in
[
23
]
[Theorem IV.3.11].

We now identify the integral form precisely.

Lemma 17.13
.

There exists a derivation
T:\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))\rightarrow\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
such that
T(X^{+}_{i,r})=X^{+}_{i,r+1}

Proof.

We define
T(X^{+}_{i,r})=X^{+}_{i,r+1}
and extend it by
T(ab):=T(a)b+aT(b)
for
a,b\in\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
and note that it satisfies the relations in Definition
17.8
and hence is well defined
24
24

24

Alternatively one can use
[
32
]
[Lemma 2.22] to construct such a operator in
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{(0)}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
.
.

∎

Definition 17.14
.

Assume
n\geq 2
. Let
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),+,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}\subset\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1}^{\pm 1},(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})^{\pm 1}]
be a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
linear sub algebra generated by
X^{+}_{i,r}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{n}^{(r)}
for
r\geq 0
such that for all
r\geq 0
, we have an extra relation

\displaystyle(\hbar_{1})(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2})\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{n}

\displaystyle=\TT^{r}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}-\hbar_{2}\mathfrak{Y}_{n})

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Y}_{2}

\displaystyle:=X^{+}_{0,0}X^{+}_{1,0}+X^{+}_{1,0}X^{+}_{0,0}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle:=[X^{+}_{0,0},X^{+}_{1,1}]+[X^{+}_{1,0},X^{+}_{0,1}]

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Y}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}X^{+}_{i,0}\cdot\left([X^{+}_{i+1,0},[X^{+}_{i+2,0},\cdots,[X^{+}_{i+n-2,0}X^{+}_{i+n-1,0}]]]\right)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}[X^{+}_{i,0},[X^{+}_{i+1,1},[X^{+}_{i+2,0},[\cdots,[X^{+}_{i+n-2,0},X^{+}_{i+n-1,0}]]]].

for
n\geq 2
.
Note that second term in the sum defining
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}
is
X^{+}_{i+1,1}
.

Note that these extra elements are defined purely via cohomological Hall algebras, but quite remarkably they satisfy interesting algebraic simplification when
\hbar_{2}=0
. See Proposition
19.2
in particular.

Theorem 17.15
.

We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
algebras

\widetilde{\mathrm{\Phi}}:\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),+,\CoHA}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}},

given by the assignment
X^{+}_{i,s}\mapsto\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}^{(s)}
and
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{K+1}\mapsto\widetilde{\gamma}_{\delta}^{(r)}
.

Proof.

The map is well defined by Proposition
17.11
, Equation
67
, and Proposition
17.7
. By Corollary
17.6
, the morphism
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Phi}}
is a surjection. The algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
module by Theorem
7.7
. After localizing,
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Phi}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]}\mathbb{C}(t_{1},t_{2})
is just the morphism
\mathrm{\Phi}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]}\mathbb{C}(t_{1},t_{2})
in the Proposition
17.11
, which is an injection. So
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Phi}}
is an injection, and we are done.
∎

18.
Application: Maulik-Okounkov Yangian for Cyclic Quiver

In this section, we calculate
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)
linear algebras Maulik-Okounkov Yangians
\mathbf{Y}^{T,\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}
in the case of cyclic quivers. We note the emphasis on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)
linearity, as the Nakajima quiver varieties are not proper, so after localizing, i.e.,
\mathbf{Y}^{T,\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}\otimes_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)}\mathrm{Frac}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt))
forgets a lot of the geometry of Nakajima quiver varieties.

18.1.
Maulik-Okounkov Yangian

Following
[
82
]
, we first quickly recall the definition of the MO Yangian. Let
Q
be any quiver and Let
T
be any torus, defined by weighting
\mathbf{w}:\overline{Q}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{r}
such that
\rho=\sum_{a\in Q_{1}}[a,a^{*}]
is homogenous. We recall that the torus associated to this is given by
\Hom(\mathbb{Z}^{r},\mathbb{C}^{*})
. In particular for
\rho
, we get a homomorphism
T\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{*}
given by
t\mapsto t(\mathbf{w}(\rho))
, which by taking pullback, defines a cohomology class
\mathbf{t}(\rho)\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)
. We define
\hbar:=\mathbf{t}(\rho)
and we assume that
\hbar\neq 0
25
25

25

The assumption
\hbar\neq 0
is crucial as only in this case MO Yangians are defined.
.Given any framing
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
, we can extend weighting
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}
to a weighting
\mathbf{w}:\overline{Q_{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{r}
defined by
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}(a)=\mathbf{w}(a)
for all
a\in\overline{Q}_{1}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}(\alpha_{i,m})=\mathbf{w}(\rho),\mathbf{w}^{\prime}(\alpha_{i,m}^{*})=0
. Let

\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q):=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\HH^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

be the equivariant cohomology of Nakajima quiver varieties, where
\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}=\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{f}}\times T
, and
\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{f}}=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{f}_{i}}
is the gauge group which reparametrize the framing. Then in
[
56
]
, by developing theory of Stable envelopes, authors define certain morphisms

R_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}}(Q)\in\mathrm{End}_{\HH_{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}_{1}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}_{1}}(Q)\otimes_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}(\pt)}\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}_{2}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}_{2}}(Q))\otimes_{\HH_{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\pt)}\mathrm{Frac}(\HH_{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\pt))

for any choice of framing
\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
, where
\mathbf{f}=\mathbf{f}_{1}+\mathbf{f}_{2}
.

By the way the theory is structured, these morphisms automatically satisfy the Yang-Baxter equations. They prove that for any
\mathbf{f}=\mathbf{f}_{1}+\mathbf{f}_{2}+\mathbf{f}_{3}
, we have

R^{12}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}}(Q)R^{13}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{3}}(Q)R^{23}_{\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{f}_{3}}(Q)=R^{23}_{\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{f}_{3}}(Q)R^{13}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{3}}(Q)R^{12}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}}(Q)

Let
S
be any ring containing
\mathbb{Q}
. Given a collection of
S
modules
F_{i}
and spectral R matrices
R_{ij}\in\mathrm{End}(F_{i}\otimes F_{j})(u)
, satisfying the Yang-Baxter Equation, there is a general way called the FRT formalism, named after Faddeev, Reshetikhin, and Takhtadzhyan, which constructs a quasi-triangular Hopf algebra which naturally acts on
F_{i}\otimes S(u)
. This was exploited by Maulik and Okounkov to construct the Maulik-Okounkov Yangian.

For any quiver
Q
, let

A^{T,\zeta^{\infty}}_{Q}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\End_{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{f}}}}\left(\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)\right).

Then the MO Yangians are defined to be a subalgebra

\mathbf{Y}^{T,\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}\subset A^{T,\zeta^{\infty}}_{Q}

defined by certain operators using above
R
matrices, as explained in
[
56
]
[Section 5.2.6]. Due to the work of Botta-Davison in
[
6
]
and Schiffmann-Vasserot in
[
79
]
, there is now an understanding of this subalgebra in terms of cohomological Hall algebras, which we now recall.

18.2.
Relation with Cohomological Hall algebras

For any Nakajima quiver variety
\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q)
, we have tautological bundles
\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
\mathcal{W}_{i}
as defined in Section
6.3
. These defines operators
\ch_{l}(\mathcal{V}_{i}),\ch_{l}(\mathcal{W}_{i})
acting by cup product on there cohomology. We see them as elements inside
A^{T}_{Q}
. In Section
13.1
, we saw that there is action of
\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{T,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
on
\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q)
, and so we have morphisms

(70)

\displaystyle\rho_{Q}\colon\mathcal{A}^{T,\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow A^{T,\zeta^{\infty}}_{Q}

(71)

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{N}}_{Q}\colon\mathcal{A}^{T,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow A^{T,\zeta^{\infty}}_{Q}

Assume that the weighting
\mathbf{w}:\overline{Q}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{r}
is defined so that it factors through weighting
\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}:\overline{Q}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{2}
given by
\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}(a)=(1,0),\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}(a^{*})=(0,1)
. Then it is proved in
[
6
]
[Corollary 1.4] that the morphism
\rho_{Q}
is an injection, while the same is proved in
[
80
]
for
\rho^{\mathcal{N}}_{Q}
. Combining Theorem 4.13 and Lemma 3.18 of
[
82
]
, and Corollary 1.4 of
[
6
]
, it follows that

Theorem 18.1
.

The Maulik-Okounkov Yangian can be identified by the subalgebra of the cohomology ring of Nakajima quiver variety generated by the image of
\rho_{Q},\rho^{\mathcal{N}}_{Q}
and tautological operators
\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
and
\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})
. i.e we have

\mathbf{Y}^{T,\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}=\langle\mathcal{A}^{T}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}},\mathcal{A}^{T,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}},\ch_{k}(\mathcal{V}_{i}),\ch_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})\rangle\subset A^{T,\zeta^{\infty}}_{Q}

as
\HH_{T}(\pt)
algebras.

We now specialize to the case when
Q=Q^{K}
is a cyclic quiver and torus
T=\mathbb{T}
.

By Remark
17.12
, we may identify
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
with the positive Half of the affine Yangian
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))
. However, in the view that the action on the Nakajima quiver variety is the right action, it is more natural to identify the nilpotent CoHA with the opposite algebra of the affine Yangian, i.e., with the negative half. This statement is also proved in
[
23
]
[Theorem III.7.3]. We have

Proposition 18.2
.

Let
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{-}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
be the negative Half of the affine Yangian, i.e. the subalgebra
\langle X^{-}_{i,r}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\geq 0\rangle
of
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
defined in Definition
2.2
. Then for any
K\geq 1
, we have an isomorphism of algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))
such that for any framing
\mathbf{f}
, this isomorphism induces left action of
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{-}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))
on
\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q^{K})
.

In Theorem
17.15
, we proved that
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is isomorphic to an integral form
\mathcal{Y}^{+,(K+1),\CoHA}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}
of the affine Yangian and thus inducing action of the integral form on
\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q^{K})
for any framing
\mathbf{f}
. It suffices to understand how the positive and negative halves glue, which we can do by the work of Varagnolo.

18.3.
Varagnolo Action of Yangian

We consider the action of the full affine Yangian on the Nakajima quiver variety, due to Varagnolo
[
92
]
, which is based on work of Nakajima in the context of equivariant
K
theory. In the case of two-parameter deformation and
K=1
, this action was also considered in
[
47
]
and in
[
23
]
. The action is defined by convolution, in a similar way to Nakajima’s action of Kac-Moody Lie algebras we considered earlier.

Let
\mathbf{f}
be any framing vector. Given two dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}_{1}
and
\mathbf{d}_{2}
,

Let
\mathbf{d}_{2}=\mathbf{d}_{1}+\delta_{i}
for some
i\in Q_{0}
. Then we define subvariety
C^{+}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})\subset\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q)\times\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{f}}(Q)
given by pairs
(\rho_{1}(a),\rho_{1}(a^{*}),\rho_{1}(\alpha_{i,l}),\rho_{1}(\alpha^{*}_{i,l}))
and
(\rho_{2}(a),\rho_{2}(a^{*}),\rho_{2}(\alpha_{i,l}),\rho_{2}(\alpha^{*}_{i,l}))
such that the representation
\rho_{2}
is quotient of representation
\rho_{1}
. It is proved in
[
66
]
that these subvarieties are Lagrangian and smooth. We can then define line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{i}
on
C^{+}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})
by
p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{i}/p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{i}
where
p_{1}:C^{+}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})\rightarrow\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q)
and
p_{2}:C^{+}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})\rightarrow\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{f}}(Q)
are the projection maps and
\mathcal{V}_{i}
are the tautological bundles at vertex
i
. Then for any
\mathbf{d}
, one define morphisms
x^{+}_{i,r}:\HH^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\rightarrow\HH^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{f}}(Q),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
by
(-1)^{(\delta_{i},\mathbf{d})}(p_{2})_{*}((c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})^{r}\cup p_{1}^{*}(\alpha))
and
x^{-}_{i,r}:\HH^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\rightarrow\HH^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d}-\delta_{i},\mathbf{f}}(Q),\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
by
(-1)^{(\delta_{i},\mathbf{d}-\delta_{i})}(p_{1})_{*}((c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})^{r}\cup p_{2}^{*}(\alpha))
.

Given any
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})
and
i\in[0,K],s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. let
h_{i,s}
be the element in the tautological ring of
\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q)
defined by

\mathrm{Res}_{z=0}\left(z^{s}\left(-1+\frac{\lambda_{-1/z}(\mathcal{F}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}))}{\lambda_{-1/z}(q_{1}q_{2}\mathcal{F}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}))}\right)\right)/(t_{1}+t_{2})

where
\mathcal{F}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})
is the element in the equivariant
K
theory
K^{T\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\mathrm{N}^{\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f}}(Q))
given by

\mathcal{F}_{i}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{f})=(q_{1}q_{2})^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{i}-(1+(q_{1}q_{2})^{-1})\mathcal{V}_{i}+q_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{V}_{i-1}+q_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{V}_{i+1}

Here
q_{1},q_{2}
are line bundles associated to action of
\mathbb{T}
, i.e
c_{1}(q_{1})=t_{1}
and
c_{1}(q_{2})=t_{2}
and
\lambda_{1/z}(V)
is the equivariant chern polynomial
\lambda_{1/z}(V)=\sum_{i\geq 0}c_{i}(V)/z^{i}
. Then we have

Theorem 18.3

(
[
92
]
)
.

For any framing
\mathbf{f}
, there is an action of affine Yangian
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(n))
on
\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q^{K})
, given by
X^{\pm}_{i,r}\mapsto x^{\pm}_{i,r}
and
H_{i,s}\mapsto h_{i,s}\cup

Thanks to Theorem
[
93
]
[Theorem 5.6], we can see the operators
x^{\pm}_{i,r}
as an action of the spherical part of CoHA.

Proposition 18.4
.

Under the morphism
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
, the Varagnolo’s action of
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))
on the
\mathrm{N}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{GL}}_{\mathbf{f}},\zeta^{\infty}}_{\mathbf{f}}(Q^{K})
coincides with
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
action defined in Section
13
. Similarly the Varagnolo’s action of
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{-}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))
can be identifed with the action of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\mathcal{N},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
under the isomorphism
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{-}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(K+1))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
.

Furthemore, by
[
23
]
[Theorem III.7.8], the subalgebra
\langle\ch_{k}(\mathcal{V}_{i}),\ch_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})\rangle\subset A^{\mathbb{T},}_{Q^{K}}
, generated by tautological classes is isomoprhic to the subalgebra
\langle h_{i,s},\ch_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})\rangle\subset A^{\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}}
. This is simply because universal polynomials exist that relate the Chern polynomial and Chern characters.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
be the subalgebra of
\mathcal{Y}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
which is generated by generators
\langle\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{+},X^{\pm}_{i,r},H_{i,r}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\geq 0\rangle
(So we only exclude the generators
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{-}
for
r\geq 0
). Let
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}=\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{n,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}[\langle k_{i,r}\rangle]
be the extended algebra, where we have added central elements
\langle k_{i,r}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},r\geq 0\rangle
to the list of generators.

Since the cup product with tautolgical classes
\ch_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})
commutes, it follows from Theorem
18.1
that there is a
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
linear algebra surjection

\mathrm{\Psi}:\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{K+1,\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}}\subset A^{\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}}

where the morphism sends
k_{i,r}\mapsto\ch_{r}(\mathcal{W}_{i})
.

Theorem 18.5
.

The morphism
\mathrm{\Psi}
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
linear algebras.

Proof.

It suffices to show that this morphism is an injection. The affine Yangian is a free
\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]
module, and so does the Maulik-Okounkov Yangian by
[
56
]
[Theorem 5.5.1]. Thus, it suffices to show that:

\mathrm{\Psi}\colon(\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{K+1,\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}})_{\mathbf{K}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}

is an isomorphism, where for any any algebra
A
,
A_{\mathbf{K}}
denote the localization
A\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]}\mathbb{C}(t_{1},t_{2})
. Clearly
(\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{K+1,\CoHA,\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}})_{\mathbf{K}}=(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}^{\mathfrak{e}}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))_{\mathbf{K}}
, where
\ddot{\mathcal{Y}^{\mathfrak{e}}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))
denote the extended affine Yangian (We add central elements
k_{i,r},i\in[0,K],r\geq 0
). Let
(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}^{0,\mathfrak{e}}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))_{\mathbf{K}}
be the subalgebra generated by generators
H_{i,s}
and
k_{i,l}
. Then it shown in
[
23
]
[Theorem III.5.6], that the extended affine Yangian
(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1)_{\mathbf{K}}
has a triangular decomposition, i.e the morphism

m:(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1)_{\mathbf{K}}\otimes(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{0,\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))_{\mathbf{K}}\otimes(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{-}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))_{\mathbf{K}}\rightarrow(\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathfrak{e}}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(K+1))_{\mathbf{K}}

is an isomorphism of vector spaces. It is proved in
[
82
]
that the Maulik-Okounkov Yangian also admits a triangular decomposition, and so we have

m:(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},+}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\otimes(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},0}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\otimes(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},-}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T}}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}},

such that there is isomorphism of algebras
(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},+}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\simeq(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})_{\mathbf{K}}
and
(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},-}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\simeq(\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})_{\mathbf{K}}
and
(\mathrm{Y}^{\mathrm{MO},\mathbb{T},0}_{Q^{K}})_{\mathbf{K}}\simeq\langle\ch_{k}(\mathcal{V}_{i}),\ch_{k}(\mathcal{W}_{i})\rangle
. Thus, the morphism
\mathrm{\Psi}_{\mathbf{K}}
restricted to each of the parts of the triangular decomposition is an isomorphism, and so we are done by the following lemma.

∎

Lemma 18.6
.

Suppose
F:A\rightarrow B
a morphism of algebras where both
A
and
B
admits a triangular decomposition
A^{+}\otimes A^{0}\otimes A^{-}\xrightarrow{m}A
,
B^{+}\otimes B^{0}\otimes B^{-}\xrightarrow{m}B
. Suppose that
F
restricts to isomorphism of algebras
F^{+}\colon A^{+}\rightarrow B^{+}
,
F^{0}\colon A^{0}\rightarrow B^{0}
and
F^{-}:A^{-}\rightarrow B^{-}
then
F
is an isomorphism of algebras.

Proof.

We have commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
