{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{+}\otimes A^{0}\otimes A^{-}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{+}\otimes B^{0}\otimes B^{-}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{+}\otimes F^{0}\otimes F^{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{B}}

and thus
F
is isomorphism of vector spaces. Since
F
is a morphism of algebras, we are done.
∎

19.
Deformed Affinized BPS Lie algebra for Cyclic Quivers

We recall that in Section
9.2
, we gave
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
a cocommutative coproduct by considering the eigenvalues of
\omega:=\sum\omega_{i}
, and we concluded that there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\bU_{\mathbb{C}[t]}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}),

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is a
\mathbb{C}[t]
linear Lie algebra, called the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
deformed affinized BPS Lie algebra. Previous results allows us to calculate the Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
explicitly. Since
\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t]
as graded vector spaces, let
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\delta}
be unique non-canonical lift of
\alpha_{i},\gamma_{\delta}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. We then have

Proposition 19.1
.

The
\mathbb{C}[t]
linear Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is generated by
u^{k}\cdot\hat{\alpha}_{i}
for all
i\in[0,K],k\geq 0
and
u^{k}\cdot\hat{\gamma}_{\delta}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

Note that since
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[t]
module, the isomorphism of algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{W}}\simeq\bU_{\mathbb{C}[t]}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}})
implies that
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[t]
module.
We define a map of free
\mathbb{C}[t]
modules

J:\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t]\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}},

defined by
J(\alpha^{(k)}_{i}f(t)):=u^{k}\cdot\hat{\alpha}_{i}
and
J(\gamma^{(k)}_{\delta})f(t))=f(t)u^{k}\cdot\hat{\gamma}_{\delta}
and extending to
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
by the Lie algebra relations on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS}_{\widetilde{Q^{{K}}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
(Theorem
16.6
). It suffices to show that the morphism
J
is surjective. Let
m_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}=(t)\subset\mathbb{C}[t]
be the maximal ideal. Then the morphism
J/(m_{\mathbb{C}^{*}})
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}
graded modules, where each graded piece is finite-dimensional and thus
J
is an isomorphism of vector spaces and so in particular surjective.
∎

We now calculate
\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}
. Since there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]}\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]/(t_{1}+t_{2}),

it follows by Theorem
17.15
, that there is an isomorphism of algebras

(\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),+,\CoHA}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}})_{t_{1}+t_{2}=0}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{C}^{*},\chi}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}.

But setting
t_{2}=\hbar
, yields

(72)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Y}^{(K+1),+,\CoHA}_{-t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}})_{t_{1}+t_{2}=0}

\displaystyle\simeq\bU_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}(S_{K+1})

and an isomorphism of Lie algebras

(73)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}

\displaystyle\simeq S_{K+1},

where
S_{K+1}
is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebra generated by
X^{+}_{i,r},r\geq 0,i\in[0,K]
and
\mathfrak{K}^{r}_{K+1},r\geq 0
with the defining relations

(74)

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{+},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]-[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i+1,s+1}]

\displaystyle=-\hbar[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]

(75)

\displaystyle[X_{i,r+1}^{+},X^{+}_{i-1,s}]-[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i-1,s+1}]

\displaystyle=0

(77)

\displaystyle=0\ \forall|i-j|>1

(78)

\displaystyle=0

(79)

\displaystyle\TT^{r}(\mathfrak{Z}_{K+1})

\displaystyle=\hbar^{2}\mathfrak{K}_{K+1}^{(r)}

when

and

(80a)

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r+1},X^{+}_{i,s}]-[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i,s+1}]

\displaystyle=0

(80b)

\displaystyle[X^{+}_{i,r+2},X^{+}_{i+1,s}]-2[X^{+}_{i,r+1},X^{+}_{i+1,s+1}]+[X^{+}_{i,r},X^{+}_{i+1,s+2}]

\displaystyle=0

when

.

The isomorphism of the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebras
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\rightarrow S_{K+1}
is given by
u^{r}\cdot\hat{\alpha}_{i}\mapsto X^{+}_{i,r}
for all

and
u^{r}\cdot\hat{\gamma}_{\delta}\mapsto\mathfrak{K}_{K+1}^{(r)},r\geq 0
. The Lie algebra
S_{K+1}
can be described more explicitly. Recall the Lie algebra
(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}
from Section
15
. We consider a derivation of
(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}
, defined by
\ad_{P_{K+1}}:=[P_{K+1},\cdot]
where

P_{K+1}=\frac{(K+1)^{2}}{2}t_{0,2}+(K+1)T_{0,1}(H^{\prime}_{K+1})+(K+1)\hbar T_{0,0}(H^{\prime\prime}_{K+1})

such that
H^{\prime}_{K+1}=\sum_{i=1}^{K+1}(K+3/2-i)E_{i,i}
and
H^{\prime\prime}_{K+1}=\sum_{i=1}^{K+1}\mu_{i}E_{i,i}
for any
\mu_{i}
satisfying
\mu_{i}-\mu_{i+1}=(1-i/(K+1))
and
\sum\mu_{i}=0
. They are chosen so that
[H^{\prime}_{K+1},E_{i,i+1}]=E_{i,i+1}
for all

,
[H^{\prime}_{K+1},E_{K+1,1}]=-K
and
[H^{\prime\prime}_{K+1},E_{i,i+1}]=(1-i/(K+1))E_{i,i+1}
for all
i\in[1,K].
This might look adhoc, but important property is that they satisfy equations
81
.

Proposition 19.2
.

We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebras

G\colon S_{K+1}\rightarrow(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}

given by

\displaystyle X^{+}_{0,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\ad_{P_{K+1}}^{r}(T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1}))

\displaystyle X^{+}_{i,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\ad_{P_{K+1}}^{r}(T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}))

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{K+1}

\displaystyle\mapsto\ad_{P_{K+1}}^{r}(t_{1,0})

Proof.

We see that in terms of matrices

(81)

\displaystyle\ad_{P_{K+1}}^{r}(T_{1,0}(E_{K+1,1}))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}(K+1)^{r}D^{r}zE_{K+1,1}

(82)

\displaystyle\ad_{P_{K+1}}^{r}(T_{0,0}(E_{i,i+1}))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}(K+1)^{r}(D+(1-i/(K+1))\hbar)^{r}E_{i,i+1}

It can be checked explictly that the defining relations (
74
)(
75
)(
76
) (
77
) (
78
) and similarly the ones for
K=1
of
S_{K+1}
are satisfied. Recall that we defined

\mathfrak{Z}_{K+1}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/(K+1)\mathbb{Z}}[X^{+}_{i,0},[X^{+}_{i+1,1},[X^{+}_{i+2,0},[\cdots,[X^{+}_{i+K-1,0},X^{+}_{i+K,0}]]]]]

Let
R_{i}=[X^{+}_{i,0},[X^{+}_{i+1,1},[X^{+}_{i+2,0},[\cdots,[X^{+}_{i+K-1,0},X^{+}_{i+K,0}]]]]]
. Then
When
i=0,K
, we calculate that

G(R_{i})=(-1)(K+1)\left(zD+(2-\frac{i+1}{K+1})\hbar z\right)(E_{i,i}-E_{i+1,i+1})

When
i=0,K
, we calculate that

\displaystyle G(R_{0})

\displaystyle=(-1)(K+1)\left(zD(E_{K+1,K+1}-E_{1,1})+\frac{\hbar zK(E_{K+1,K+1}-E_{1,1}}{K+1}-\hbar zE_{1,1}\right)

\displaystyle G(R_{K})

\displaystyle=(-1)(K+1)\left(zD(E_{K,K}-E_{K+1,K+1})+\hbar z(E_{K,K}-E_{K+1,K+1})\right)

Then summing up them up gives

G(\mathfrak{Z}_{K+1})=\sum_{i=0}^{K}G(R_{i})=\hbar z\mathrm{Id}

Thus
G(\mathfrak{Z}_{K+1})=\hbar^{2}t_{1,0}
, thus relations
79
and
80d
are satisfied for
r=0
. But note that
G(T(X^{+}_{i,r}))=G(X^{+}_{i,r+1})=\ad_{P_{K+1}}(G(X_{i,r}))
thus these relations are also satisfied for
r>1
. It is a surjective map of Lie algebras since
(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}
is generated by
t_{1,r},r\geq 0
and
T_{0,r}(E_{i,i+1}),T_{1,r}(E_{K+1,1})
, all of which are inductively in the image of
G
. To show the injection, we note that
G
respects both cohomological and dimension grading on
S_{K+1}
, induced by the isomorphism
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq S_{K+1}
. It is easy to check by Proposition
8.2
that the graded dimensions of
S_{K+1}
and
(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}
are finite dimensional and are the same. Thus
G
is, in fact, an isomorphism. Alternatively, it is an injective map after localizing with
\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{-1}]
since then
G[\hbar^{-1}]
is the positive half of the isomorphism of Lie algebras considered in
[
88
]
[Theorem 2.2].
∎

Combining Proposition
19.2
and Equation (
73
) implies

Theorem 19.3
.

There is an isomorphism of of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear Lie algebras

\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\BPS,\mathbb{C}^{*}}_{\widetilde{Q^{K}},\widetilde{W^{K}}}\simeq(\mathcal{W}_{K+1})^{+}

19.1.
Relation with algebras of Gaiotto-Rapčák-Zhou

In
[
76
]
, a procedure to do the Drinfeld double of the spherical part of CoHA is explained. Then, a certain quotient of the Drinfeld double gives rise to
reduced
Drinfeld double
[
93
]
[Section 4.1]. Mimicking the same construction of reduced Drinfeld double to
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),+,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
yields the algebra
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
for
n>2
, defined in Definition
2.2
26
26

26

More precisely, we apply the construction to the localized algebra (which is spherically generated due to Proposition
17.7.2
and
17.11
) and take the minimal subalgebra containing both
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),+,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
and
(\mathcal{Y}^{(n),-,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}):=(\mathcal{Y}^{(n),+,\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}})^{\textrm{op}}
.
. Then in an analogous way to
[
33
]
, we define

Definition 19.4

(CoHA Loop Yangian)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
be the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
algebra, defined to be the quotient
\mathcal{Y}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}/(\textbf{c})
where
\textbf{c}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i,0}

We then have a doubled version of the Proposition

19.2
, which follows from work of Tsymbaliuk
[
88
]
who calculated the classical limit of affine Yangians.

Proposition 19.5
.

We have an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
linear algebras

\mathcal{L}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar,\hbar_{2}}/(\hbar_{2})\simeq\bU_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}(\mathcal{W}_{n}).

Proof.

We use the morphism considered in
[
88
]
[Theorem 2.2]
27
27

27

The codomain of the morphism considered in
[
88
]
[Theorem 2.2] is a central extension, however in the proof, they consider the morphism induced after quotienting by the central extension.
whose positive half is the morphism we considered in Theorem
19.2
i.e., we define a morphism of algebras

\Psi:\mathcal{L}^{(n),\CoHA}_{\hbar,\hbar_{2}}/(\hbar_{2})\rightarrow\bU_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}(\mathcal{W}_{n})

which sends

\displaystyle X^{+}_{0,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}D^{r}z\otimes E_{n,1}

\displaystyle X^{+}_{i,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}(D+(1-i/n)\hbar)^{r}\otimes E_{i,i+1}

\displaystyle X^{-}_{0,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}(z^{-1}D^{r})\otimes E_{1,n}

\displaystyle X^{-}_{i,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}(D+(1-i/n)\hbar)^{r}\otimes E_{i+1,i}

\displaystyle H_{0,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}(D^{r}\otimes E_{n,n}-(D+\hbar)^{r}\otimes E_{1,1})

\displaystyle H_{i,r}

\displaystyle\mapsto n^{r}(D+(1-i/n)\hbar)^{r}\otimes(E_{i,i}-E_{i+1,i+1}).

We haven’t defined image of
\mathfrak{K}^{(r)}_{\pm}
, but note that we can still calculate image of elements
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}
, and we can check that this map must send
\mathfrak{K}^{(0)}_{+}
to
\frac{z}{\hbar}=T_{1,0}(1)/\hbar=t_{1,0}
while
\mathfrak{K}^{0}_{-}
to
-\frac{z^{-1}}{\hbar}=-T_{-1,0}(1)/\hbar=-t_{-1,0}
which allows to extend define using the same trick as in proof of Proposition
19.2
. After inverting
\hbar
, this map is an isomorphism by
[
88
]
[Theorem 2.2] while it is a surjection, since again
D_{\hbar}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\otimes\widetilde{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
is generated by
t_{1,r},t_{-1,r},T_{-1,r}(E_{1,n}),T_{0,r}(E_{i,i+1}),T_{0,r}(E_{i+1,i}),T_{1,r}(E_{n,1})
all of which can be easily seen to be in the image.
∎

We consider the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
linear algebras

\epsilon_{1}

\epsilon_{2}

.

Y^{K}
and its loop version

, as defined in

\mathcal{A}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\widetilde{Q_{\Jor}},\widetilde{W}}\simeq\ddot{\mathcal{Y}}^{+}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}(\mathfrak{gl}(1)).

Y^{K}\simeq\mathcal{Y}^{(K),\CoHA}_{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}}
